Fig 1:
Illustration of the lower bound construction of
P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
in the proof of Proposition
14
. Blue and red regions correspond to the regression function
\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
being above and below
\tau
respectively. Note the different behaviour in the
\ell
th region
K_{r}^{0}(\ell)
from the others. The marginal measure
\mu_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is uniformly distributed on
\bigcup_{\ell\in[L]}\bigl(K_{r}^{0}(\ell)\cup K_{r}^{1}(\ell)\bigr)
; the boundaries of these regions are denoted with black lines.

Lemmas
S28
and
S30
verify that
\bigl\{P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}:\ell\in[L]\bigr\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
for appropriate choices of
r
,
w
,
s
and
\theta
. Moreover, Proposition
S32
reveals both that the chi-squared divergences between pairs of distributions in our construction are small, and yet that the distributions are sufficiently different that any set
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}})\bigr)
for distinct
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
must have much smaller
\mu
-measure than
M_{\tau}
. To conclude, we apply a constrained risk inequality due to
Brown and Low 1996
in the proof of Proposition
14
(i) and a version of Fano’s lemma in the proof of Proposition
14
(ii).

Proposition
15
provides the final (parametric) part of the lower bounds in Theorems
2
and
11
.

Proposition 15
.

Take
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1/2]
,
\tau\in(\epsilon_{0},1-\epsilon_{0})

(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,\infty)\times[1,\infty)
,
(\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,C_{\mathrm{App}})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times(0,1)\times[1,\infty)
with
\beta\gamma(\kappa-1)<d\kappa
and
\upsilon\in(0,4^{-d}]
. Suppose that
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfies
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{conv}}
. Then there exists
c_{2}>0
, depending only on
\epsilon_{0}
,
d
,
\beta
,
\kappa
,
\gamma
,
\lambda
and
C_{\mathrm{App}}
, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in(0,1/2-\epsilon_{0}]
and
\hat{A}\in\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}\bigl(\tau,\alpha,\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})\bigr)
, we can find
P\in\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
with regression function
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[\tau-\epsilon_{0}/2,\tau+\epsilon_{0}/2]
and marginal distribution
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
that satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl[\bigl\{M_{\tau}(P,\mathcal{A})-\mu(\hat{A})\bigr\}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}}\Bigr]\geq\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

The construction for the proof of Proposition
15
is somewhat different from those in the proof of Proposition
14
and is illustrated in Figure
2
: it hinges on the difficulty of estimating
\mu(A)
for
A\in\mathcal{A}
. To formalise this idea, given
t\in[1,\infty)
,
\theta\in(0,\epsilon_{0}/2]
,
s\in(0,1]
and
\zeta\in\bigl[0,\frac{s^{d}}{2\{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}\}}\bigr]
, we first define a pair of distributions
\{P_{t,\theta,s,\zeta}^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\{-1,1\}}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]
. Define
\eta\equiv\eta_{t,\theta,s}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
by

\displaystyle\eta(x)\equiv\eta_{t,\theta,s}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}):=\begin{cases}\tau+{\theta}&\text{ for }x_{1}\leq-t-s\\
\tau+\theta\bigl\{1-2h\bigl(\frac{-x_{1}-t}{s}\bigr)\bigr\}&\text{ for }-t-s<x_{1}\leq-t\\
\tau-{\theta}&\text{ for }-t<x_{1}\leq t\\
\tau+\theta\bigl\{1-2h\bigl(\frac{x_{1}-t}{s}\bigr)\bigr\}&\text{ for }t<x_{1}\leq t+s\\
\tau+\theta&\text{ for }x_{1}\geq t+s.\end{cases}

Define
A_{0}:=[-t,t]^{d}
,
A_{-1}:=[-t-2s,-t-s]\times[-\frac{s}{2},\frac{s}{2}]^{d-1}
and
A_{1}:=[t+s,t+2s]\times[-\frac{s}{2},\frac{s}{2}]^{d-1}
. For
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
, let
\mu^{\ell}_{\zeta}\equiv\mu_{t,s,\zeta}^{\ell}
be the Lebesgue-absolutely continuous measure supported on
A_{-1}\cup A_{0}\cup A_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
with piecewise constant density
f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
given by

\displaystyle f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}}(x):=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}+\frac{\zeta\cdot j\cdot\ell}{s^{d}}&\text{ for }x\in A_{j}\text{ with }j\in\{-1,0,1\},\\
0&\text{ for }x\notin A_{-1}\cup A_{0}\cup A_{1}.\end{cases}

Now for
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
, let
P_{\zeta}^{\ell}\equiv P_{t,\theta,s,\zeta}^{\ell}
denote the unique distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}
with marginal
\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and regression function
\eta
. Figure
2
illustrates this construction.

A_{0}
A_{-1}
A_{1}

Fig 2:
Illustration of the lower bound construction of
P_{\zeta}^{1}\equiv P_{t,\theta,s,\zeta}^{1}
in the proof of Proposition
15
. Blue and red regions correspond to the regression function
\eta_{t,\theta,s}
being above and below
\tau
respectively. The density of dots is greatest on
A_{1}
and smallest on
A_{-1}
, reflecting the greater marginal density of
\mu_{\zeta}^{1}
on
A_{1}
; for
P_{\zeta}^{-1}
, the density of dots would be reversed.

In the proof of Proposition
15
, we will show that
\bigl\{P_{t,\theta,s,\zeta}^{\ell}:\ell\in\{-1,1\}\bigr\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
for suitable
t
,
\theta
,
s
and
\zeta
. Moreover,
P_{t,\theta,s,\zeta}^{-1}
and
P_{t,\theta,s,\zeta}^{1}
are close in chi-squared divergence, but nevertheless we cannot have both
\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}(A)
and
\mu_{\zeta}^{1}(A)
close to
M_{\tau}(P_{\zeta}^{-1},\mathcal{A})=M_{\tau}(P_{\zeta}^{1},\mathcal{A})
for
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
. Hence, any data-dependent selection set
\hat{A}
that satisfies our Type I error guarantee must incur large regret for at least one of these distributions.

6
Application to study of heterogeneous treatment effects

The aim of this section is to show how our previous results may be applied to the two-arm setting with a treatment and control, where we are interested in regions of substantial treatment effect. To this end, let
\tilde{P}
denote the distribution of a random triple
(X,T,\tilde{Y})
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\times[0,1]
, where
X
represents covariates,
T
is a treatment indicator and
\tilde{Y}
denotes the corresponding response. Assume that
X\sim\mu
, and that the function
\pi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
given by
\pi(x):=\mathbb{P}(T=1|X=x)
is known. For
\ell\in\{0,1\}
, let
\tilde{\eta}^{\ell}(x):=\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}|X=x,T=\ell)
. The
heterogeneous treatment effect
is the function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[-1,1]
defined by
\varphi(x):=\tilde{\eta}^{1}(x)-\tilde{\eta}^{0}(x)
for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Given
t\in[-1,1]
and a class of sets
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, our primary interest is in identifying subsets
A\in\mathcal{A}
that are contained in
\mathcal{X}_{t}(\varphi):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\varphi(x)\geq t\}
based on data
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}:=\bigl((X_{1},T_{1},\tilde{Y}_{1}),\ldots,(X_{n},T_{n},\tilde{Y}_{n})\bigr)\sim\tilde{P}^{\otimes n}
.

Given a family
{\mathcal{P}}
of distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\times[0,1]
and a significance level
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we let
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}^{\mathrm{HTE}}(t,\alpha,\mathcal{P})
denote the set of functions
\hat{A}:(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\times[0,1])^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
such that
(x,\tilde{D})\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{A}(\tilde{D})}(x)
is a Borel measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\times[0,1])^{n}
and we have the Type I error guarantee that

\inf_{\tilde{P}\in{\mathcal{P}}}\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{P}}\bigl(\hat{A}(\tilde{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{t}(\varphi)\bigr)\geq 1-\alpha.

Similarly to our formulation in Section
2
, we seek
\hat{A}\in\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}^{\mathrm{HTE}}(t,\alpha,{\mathcal{P}})
with low regret

R_{t}^{\varphi}(\hat{A}):=\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{t}(\varphi)\bigr)\bigr\}-\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{P}}\big\{\mu\bigl(\hat{A}(\tilde{\mathcal{D}})\bigr)\bigm|\hat{A}(\tilde{\mathcal{D}})\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{t}(\varphi)\big\}.

Given
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,\infty)\times[1,\infty)
and a Borel measurable function
\pi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
, we let
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{HTE}}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\pi)
denote the class of distributions
\tilde{P}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\times[0,1]
such that
\varphi
is
(\beta,\lambda)
-Hölder (see Definition
4
), and such that
\pi(x)=\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{P}}(T=1|X=x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Similarly, given
(\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,C_{\mathrm{App}})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times(0,1)\times[1,\infty)
, we let
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{HTE}}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},t,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,C_{\mathrm{App}})
denote the class of all distributions
\tilde{P}
such that
\mu
is absolutely continuous, with Lebesgue density
f_{\mu}
, and such that (
16
) holds with
\varphi
in place of
\eta
, with
t
in place of
\tau
, and with
\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{t}(\varphi)\bigr)\bigr\}
in place of
M_{\tau}
.

The following result on the minimax rate of regret in this heterogeneous treatment effect context is an almost immediate corollary of Theorem
11
.

Corollary 16
.

Take
\zeta_{0}\in(0,1/2)
,
t\in\bigl[-(1-{\zeta_{0}}),1-{\zeta_{0}}\bigr]
,
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,\infty)\times[1,\infty)
,
(\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,C_{\mathrm{App}})\in(0,\infty)^{2}\times(0,1)\times[1,\infty)
with
\beta\gamma(\kappa-1)<d\kappa
and
\upsilon\in\bigl(0,(4d^{1/2})^{-d}\bigr]
, and let
\pi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[\zeta_{0},1-\zeta_{0}]
be Borel measurable. Let
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfy
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{conv}}
,
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})<\infty
and
\emptyset\in\mathcal{A}
. Given
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in(0,1/2-\zeta_{0}]
, we have

\inf_{\hat{A}}\,\sup_{\tilde{P}}\,R_{t}^{\varphi}(\hat{A})\asymp\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\log_{+}(n/\alpha)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}+\frac{1}{n^{1/2}},1\biggr\},

(23)

where the infimum in (
23
) is taken over
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}^{\mathrm{HTE}}\bigl(t,\alpha,\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{HTE}}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\pi)\bigr)
, the supremum is taken over
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{HTE}}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\pi)\cap\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{HTE}}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},t,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,C_{\mathrm{App}})
. In (
23
),
\asymp
indicates that the ratio of the left- and right-hand sides is bounded above and below by positive quantities depending only on
d
,
\beta
,
\lambda
,
\kappa
,
\gamma
,
\upsilon
,
C_{\mathrm{App}}
,
\zeta_{0}
and
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})
.

To establish the upper bound in Corollary
16
, we reduce the problem to the setting of Section
4
by letting
\rho_{\min}:=\min\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(x),1-\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(x)\}
and introducing proxy labels

\displaystyle Y:=\frac{1}{2}\biggl\{1+\frac{\rho_{\min}}{\pi(X)(1-\pi(X))}\cdot\bigl(T-\pi(X)\bigr)\tilde{Y}\biggr\},

so that
Y
takes values in
[0,1]
and satisfies both
\eta(x):=\mathbb{E}(Y|X=x)=\frac{1}{2}\bigl(1+\rho_{\min}\cdot\varphi(x)\bigr)
and
\mathcal{X}_{t}(\varphi)=\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
with
\tau:=\frac{1}{2}(1+\rho_{\min}\cdot t)
. The upper bound then follows from Theorem
11
(i).

To deduce the lower bound, we convert distributions
P
of random pairs
(X,Y)
into distributions
\tilde{P}
of random triples
(X,T,\tilde{Y})
for which
\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{P}}(T=1|X=x,Y=y)=\pi(x)
and
\tilde{Y}:=T\cdot Y+(1-T)\cdot(1-Y)
, so that the corresponding heterogeneous treatment effect satisfies
\varphi(x)=2\eta(x)-1
. We may therefore deduce the lower bound in Corollary
16
from Theorem
11
(ii), applied with
\tau:=(1+t)/2
.

S1
Proof of the hardness result

Fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and let
(P_{x})_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
be a disintegration of
P
into conditional probability measures on
[0,1]
; see Section
S8.1
and Lemma
S34
. Since
\mathcal{A}
has finite VC dimension, it follows from the Vapnik–Chervonenkis concentration inequality (Lemma
S36
) that there exists a finite set
\mathbb{T}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
for which

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\bigg|\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{t\in A\}}-\mu(A)\bigg|\leq\epsilon.

(S1)

Since
\mathbb{T}
is finite and
\mu
has no atoms, we may choose a radius
r>0
sufficiently small that
\mu\bigl(\bigcup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}B_{r}(t)\bigr)\leq\epsilon
. Now define a function
\rho:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
by
\rho(x):=1\wedge\bigwedge_{t\in\mathbb{T}}\{(2/r)\cdot\|x-t\|_{\infty}\}
, noting that
\rho
is Lipschitz. Further, define a family of probability distributions
(Q_{x})_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
on
[0,1]
by

\int_{[0,1]}h(y)\,dQ_{x}(y)=\int_{[0,1]}h\bigl(\rho(x)\cdot y\bigr)\,dP_{x}(y),

for all Borel functions
h:[0,1]\rightarrow[0,1]
, and define a probability distribution
Q
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]
by
Q(A\times B)=\int_{A}Q_{x}(B)\,d\mu(x)
. It follows that
(Q_{x})_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is a disintegration of
Q
into conditional probability measures on
[0,1]
. In addition, taking a random pair
(X^{Q},Y^{Q})\sim Q
we see by (
S40
) that for
\mu
-almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\eta_{Q}(x)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(Y^{Q}\mid X^{Q}=x\bigr)=\int_{[0,1]}y\,dQ_{x}(y)=\rho(x)\cdot\int_{[0,1]}y\,dP_{x}(y)=\rho(x)\cdot\eta_{P}(x).

Hence, we may extend the definition of
\eta_{Q}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d}
in such a way that
\eta_{Q}(\cdot)=\rho(\cdot)\eta_{P}(\cdot)
, which is a product of Lipschitz functions, so is itself Lipschitz; thus,
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\mu)
. Note also that for every
t\in\mathbb{T}
, we have
\eta_{Q}(t)=\rho(t)\eta_{P}(t)=0<\tau
, so
\mathbb{T}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{Q}})=\emptyset
. Moreover,
\eta_{Q}(x)\leq\eta_{P}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, so
\mathcal{X}_{\tau}{(\eta_{Q})}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{P})
. Hence, since
\hat{A}
controls the Type I error at the level
\alpha
over
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Lip}}(\mu)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{Q}\bigl\{\hat{A}\subseteq{}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\mathbb{T})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}{(\eta_{P})}\bigr\}\geq\mathbb{P}_{Q}\bigl\{\hat{A}\subseteq{}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\mathbb{T})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}{(\eta_{Q})}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{Q}\bigl\{\hat{A}\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}{(\eta_{Q})}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\alpha.

(S2)

Now
Q_{x}=P_{x}
for
x\notin\bigcup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}B_{r}(t)
. Hence

\mathrm{H}^{2}(P,Q)\leq 2\mathrm{TV}(P,Q)\leq 2\mu\biggl(\bigcup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}B_{r}(t)\biggr)\leq 2\epsilon,

so

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}^{2}(P^{\otimes n},Q^{\otimes n})\leq\mathrm{H}^{2}(P^{\otimes n},Q^{\otimes n})=2\biggl\{1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\biggl(1-\frac{\mathrm{H}^{2}(P,Q)}{2}\biggr)\biggr\}\leq 2\bigl(1-(1-\epsilon)^{n}\bigr).

(S3)

Note that by (
S1
) if
A\in\mathcal{A}
satisfies
A\cap\mathbb{T}=\emptyset
, then
\mu(A)\leq\epsilon
. Hence, by (
S1
) and (
S3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\bigl\{\mu(\hat{A})\leq\epsilon\bigr\}\cap\bigl\{\hat{A}\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}\subseteq{}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\mathbb{T}\bigr)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}{(\eta_{P})}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\alpha-\sqrt{2\bigl(1-(1-\epsilon)^{n}\bigr)}.

Letting
\epsilon\searrow 0
gives (
3
).
Thus,

\displaystyle R_{\tau}(\hat{A})=M_{\tau}-\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl({\mu}(\hat{A})\mid\hat{A}\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\geq(1-\alpha)\cdot M_{\tau}.

Finally, note that for any
\xi>0
, we may take
A_{\xi}\in\mathcal{A}
with
\mu(A_{\xi})>M_{\tau}-\xi
and
A_{\xi}\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
. Hence, we may define
\bar{A}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}
that takes the value
A_{\xi}
with probability
\alpha
and
\emptyset
otherwise; it has regret
R_{\tau}(\hat{A})<(1-\alpha)\cdot M_{\tau}+\alpha\cdot\xi
. Letting
\xi\searrow 0
yields the final equality in (
4
).
∎

S2
Proof of the upper bound in Theorem
2

Recall that Theorem
2
(i) will follow from Lemma
3
, together with Propositions
4
and
5
.

We begin by showing that
\sup_{x\in B}\eta(x)\leq t:=\tau+\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B)^{\beta}
. Indeed, suppose for a contradiction that there exists some
x_{0}\in B
with
\eta(x_{0})>t
. Since
B\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
there also exists
x_{1}\in B
with
\eta(x_{1})\leq\tau
. Since
\eta
is continuous there exists
x_{2}
on the line segment between
x_{0}
and
x_{1}
with
\eta(x_{2})=\tau
. Thus, since
x_{0}
,
x_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
we have,

\displaystyle\eta(x_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\eta(x_{2})+|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{2})|\leq\tau+\lambda\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{2}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\tau+\lambda\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}\leq\tau+\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B)^{\beta}=t<\eta(x_{0}),

a contradiction which proves the claim
\sup_{x\in B}\eta(x)\leq t
. Now let
m:=n\cdot\hat{\mu}_{n}(B)=\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{i}\in B\}}
. If
m=0
, then
\hat{p}_{n}(B)=1
, so we may assume without loss of generality that
m\geq 1
. Let
(i_{j})_{j\in[m]}
denote a strictly increasing sequence such that
X_{i_{j}}\in B
for all
j\in[m]
. For each
j\in[m]
let
Z_{j}:=Y_{i_{j}}
so that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big(Z_{j}\mid\mathcal{D}_{X}\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\big(Y_{i_{j}}\mid\mathcal{D}_{X}\big)=\eta(X_{i_{j}})\leq t.

Moreover,
(Z_{j})_{j\in[m]}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{D}_{X}
. Writing
\bar{Z}:=m^{-1}\sum_{j\in[m]}Z_{j}
, we have by construction of
\hat{p}_{n}(B)
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\hat{p}_{n}(B)\leq\alpha\mid\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigr)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\Bigl[\exp\bigl\{-n\cdot\hat{\mu}_{n}(B)\cdot\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\hat{\eta}_{n}(B),t\bigr)\bigr\}\leq\alpha\text{ and }\hat{\eta}_{n}(B)>t\Bigm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\Bigr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\bigl\{\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\bar{Z},t\bigr)\geq m^{-1}\cdot{\log(1/\alpha)}\text{ and }\bar{Z}>t\bigm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigr\}\leq\alpha,

where the final inequality follows from a Chernoff bound, stated for convenience as Lemma
S38
.
∎The proof of Proposition
4
will rely on the following lemma.

Lemma S1
.

Fix
\beta\in(0,1]
,
\lambda\geq 1
and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\tau)
with
\eta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}\bigl(\beta,\lambda,\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
. Then, with
(B_{(\ell)})_{\ell\in[\hat{L}]}
,
\ell_{\alpha}
and
m
as in Algorithm
1
(and setting
\ell_{\alpha}:=0
when
\hat{L}\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{(1)})>\alpha
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\biggr)\leq\alpha.

Let

\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{D}_{X}):=\{B\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D}_{X}):B\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}
and
K:=|\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{D}_{X})|
. Note that

\biggl\{\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\biggr\}\bigcap\{K=0\}\subseteq\biggl\{\bigcup_{\ell\in[\hat{L}]}B_{(\ell)}\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\biggr\}\bigcap\{K=0\}=\emptyset.

On the other hand, when
K\geq 1
, we may write

\tilde{\ell}:=\min\bigl\{\ell\in[\hat{L}]~:~B_{(\ell)}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{D}_{X})\bigr\},

so that when
\hat{L}\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{(1)})\leq\alpha
, we have
\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
if and only if
\tilde{\ell}\leq\ell_{\alpha}
. When
K\geq 1
, we have by the minimality of
\tilde{\ell}
that
\tilde{\ell}\leq\hat{L}+1-K
, so

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)

\displaystyle\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\biggr)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\biggl\{\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\not\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\biggr\}\bigcap\{\hat{L}\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{(1)})\leq\alpha\}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\biggr)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\big(\{\tilde{\ell}\leq\ell_{\alpha}\}\cap\{\hat{L}\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{(1)})\leq\alpha\}\bigm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\big(\bigl\{(\hat{L}+1-\tilde{\ell})\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{(\tilde{\ell})})\leq\alpha\bigr\}\cap\{\hat{L}\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{(1)})\leq\alpha\}\bigm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(K\cdot\min_{B\in\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{D}_{X})}\hat{p}_{n}(B)\leq\alpha\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{B\in\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{D}_{X})}{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\hat{p}_{n}(B)\leq\frac{\alpha}{K}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\biggr)\leq\alpha,

where we applied Lemma
3
for the final inequality.
∎

By construction in Algorithm
1
, we have
\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}
. Hence the result follows from Lemma
S1
.
∎We now turn to the proof of Proposition
5
. A key component of this result is the following proposition, which states that if a set
A\in\mathcal{A}
may be covered with a finite collection of hyper-cubes
\{B_{1},\ldots,B_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
, each with sufficiently large diameter and
\mu
-measure, in such a way that
\eta
is well above the level
\tau
on each
B_{\ell}
, then
\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}
will return a set of
\mu
-measure comparable with
\mu(A)
.

Proposition S2
.

Take
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,1]\times(0,\infty)
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
and
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})<\infty
and
\emptyset\in\mathcal{A}
. Given
L\in\mathbb{N}
, suppose that there exist hyper-cubes
\{B_{1},\ldots,B_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
such that
\min_{q\in[L]}\mu(B_{q})\geq 8\log(4L/\delta)/n
,
\min_{q\in[L]}\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})\geq 1/n
and

\displaystyle\min_{q\in[L]}\biggl\{\sup_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-2\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})^{\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log\bigl(2^{2+d}L\cdot n(2+\log_{2}n)/(\alpha\cdot\delta)\bigr)}{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}}\biggr\}\geq\tau.

(S4)

Let
S^{\dagger}:=\bigcup_{q\in[L]}B_{q}
, and taking the universal constant
C_{\mathrm{VC}}>0
from Lemma
S36
, let

\displaystyle J_{n,\delta}(S^{\dagger}):=\sup\big\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}(S^{\dagger})\big\}-2C_{\mathrm{VC}}~\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}.

Then

\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\mu\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}(\mathcal{D})\bigr)<J_{n,\delta}(S^{\dagger})\bigr\}\leq\delta.

Proposition
S2
will be proved through a series of lemmas below.

Lemma S3
.

Let
P
be a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]
having marginal
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
\delta\in(0,1)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose further that
\{B_{1},\ldots,B_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
with
\min_{q\in[L]}\mu(B_{q})\geq 8\log(4L/\delta)/n
, and define the event

\mathcal{E}_{1,\delta}:=\biggl\{\min_{q\in[L]}\biggl(\hat{\mu}_{n}(B_{q})-\frac{\mu(B_{q})}{2}\biggr)>0\biggr\}.

Then
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{1,\delta}^{\mathrm{c}})\leq\delta/4
.

By the multiplicative Chernoff bound (Lemma
S39
), for each
q\in[L]
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\hat{\mu}_{m}(B_{q})\leq\frac{\mu(B_{q})}{2}\biggr)={\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{i}\in B_{q}\}}\leq\frac{m}{2}\cdot\mu(B_{q})\biggr)\leq\exp\biggl(-\frac{m}{8}\cdot\mu(B_{q})\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta}{4L}.

The result therefore follows by a union bound.
∎

Lemma S4
.

Let
P
be a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]
having regression function
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
, and let
\delta\in(0,1)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
\{B_{1},\ldots,B_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
and define the event

\mathcal{E}_{2,\delta}:=\biggl\{\max_{q\in[L]}\biggl(\inf_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-\hat{\eta}_{n}(B_{q})-\sqrt{\frac{\log(4L/\delta)}{2n\cdot\hat{\mu}_{n}(B_{q})}}\biggr)<0\biggr\},

where the empirical distribution
\hat{\mu}_{n}
and empirical regression function
\hat{\eta}_{n}
are defined in (
7
) and (
8
) respectively. Then
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}_{2,\delta}^{\mathrm{c}})\leq\delta/4
.

By Hoeffding’s inequality (Lemma
S38
), for every
q\in[L]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{ess\hskip 1.42262ptsup}~{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\hat{\eta}_{n}(B_{q})\leq\inf_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-\sqrt{\frac{\log(4L/\delta)}{2n\cdot\hat{\mu}_{n}(B_{q})}}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta}{4L}.

The result now follows by the law of total expectation, combined with a union bound.
∎

Lemma S5
.

Let
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,1]\times[1,\infty)
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
L\in\mathbb{N}
and
\xi\in(0,1)
. Suppose that for some
\{B_{1},\ldots,B_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
we have
\min_{q\in[L]}\mu(B_{q})\geq 8\log(8L/\delta)/n
and

\displaystyle\min_{q\in[L]}\biggl\{\sup_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-2\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})^{\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log\bigl(4L/(\xi\cdot\delta)\bigr)}{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}}\biggr\}\geq\tau.

(S5)

Then, recalling the definition of the
p
-values
\hat{p}_{n}
from (
9
), we have

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\max_{q\in[L]}~\hat{p}_{n}(B_{q})\geq\xi\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

By Lemmas
S3
and
S4
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1,\delta}^{\mathrm{c}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{2,\delta}^{\mathrm{c}})\leq\delta/2
. On
\mathcal{E}_{1,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2,\delta}
, we have for each
q\in[L]
that

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{n}(B_{q})

\displaystyle>\inf_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-\sqrt{\frac{\log(4L/\delta)}{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})^{\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{\log(4L/\delta)}{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}}

\displaystyle\geq\tau+\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})^{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\xi)}{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}},

where we used the fact that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
, (
S5
) and the fact that
\sqrt{2(a+b)}\geq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for all
a,b\geq 0
. Thus, on
\mathcal{E}_{1,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2,\delta}
, we have for every
q\in[L]
that

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{n}(B_{q})\leq\exp\biggl(-\frac{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}{2}\cdot\mathrm{kl}\bigl\{\hat{\eta}_{n}(B_{q}),\tau+\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})^{\beta}\bigr\}\biggr)<\xi,

as required, where the final bound uses Pinsker’s inequality.
∎

We can now complete the proof of Proposition
S2
before returning to complete the proof of Proposition
5
.

We begin by defining events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}

\displaystyle:=\biggl\{\max_{q\in[L]}\hat{p}_{n}(B_{q})<\frac{\alpha}{2^{d}n(2+\log_{2}n)}\biggr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{VC}}

\displaystyle:=\biggl\{\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\big|\hat{\mu}_{n}(A)-\mu(A)\big|\leq C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}\biggr\}.

By Lemma
S5
, with
\xi=\alpha/\bigl(2^{d}n(2+\log_{2}n)\bigr)\in(0,1)
, and Lemma
S36
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}^{\mathrm{c}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{VC}}^{\mathrm{c}})\leq\delta
. On
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}
we have each
\hat{p}_{n}(B_{q})<1
, which implies
\hat{\mu}_{n}(B_{q})>0
, and since
\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{1})\geq 1/n
we deduce that
B_{q}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
.

Now
\hat{L}=|\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{D}_{X})|\leq 2^{d}n(2+\log_{2}n)
, so on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}
, for each
q\in[L]
, we have
\hat{L}\cdot\hat{p}_{n}(B_{q})\leq\alpha
, and hence
B_{q}=B_{(\ell(q))}
for some
\ell(q)\leq\ell_{\alpha}
. Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}
we have
S^{\dagger}\subseteq\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}
. Now take
\zeta>0
and choose
A_{\zeta}^{*}\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}(S^{\dagger})
with
\mu(A^{*}_{\zeta})>\sup\big\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}(S^{\dagger})\big\}-\zeta
. It follows that
A_{\zeta}^{*}\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\bigr)
and hence on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{VC}}
that

\displaystyle\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})

\displaystyle\geq\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})-C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}

\displaystyle\geq\hat{\mu}_{n}(A^{*}_{\zeta})-C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}

\displaystyle\geq{\mu}(A^{*}_{\zeta})-2C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}\geq J_{n,\delta}(S^{\dagger})-\zeta.

Letting
\zeta\searrow 0
, we conclude that
\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})\geq J_{n,\delta}(S^{\dagger})
, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{PV}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{VC}}
, as required.
∎

We define
\rho:=\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma
,

\displaystyle\theta:=\frac{8\lambda^{d/\beta}}{n}\log_{+}\biggl(\frac{4\cdot 3^{d}\cdot n}{\alpha\wedge\delta}\biggr),

r_{*}:=\lambda^{-1/\beta}\theta^{\kappa/\rho}
,
\xi:=\theta^{\beta\gamma/\rho}
and
\Delta:=2^{4}\theta^{\beta\kappa/\rho}
. We initially assume that
\Delta\leq 1
, so that
1/n\leq\lambda^{-d/\beta}\cdot\theta\leq r_{*}\leq 2^{-4}
. Now choose a maximal subset
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)
with the property that
\|x_{q}-x_{q^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}>r_{*}
for distinct
q,q^{\prime}\in[L]
. Then
\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\subseteq\bigcup_{q\in[L]}\bar{B}_{r_{*}}(x_{q})
and
\bar{B}_{r_{*}/3}(x_{q})\cap\bar{B}_{r_{*}/3}(x_{q^{\prime}})=\emptyset
for distinct
q,q^{\prime}\in[L]
. Now, since
\xi\leq 1
,

\displaystyle L\leq\sum_{q=1}^{L}\frac{\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{*}/3}(x_{q})\bigr)}{\xi\cdot(r_{*}/3)^{d}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\xi\cdot(r_{*}/3)^{d}}\cdot\mu\biggl(\bigcup_{q=1}^{L}\bar{B}_{r_{*}/3}(x_{q})\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(3\lambda^{1/\beta})^{d}}{\theta^{(\beta\gamma+d\kappa)/\rho}}\leq\frac{3^{d}\lambda^{d/\beta}}{\theta}\leq 3^{d}n.

For each
q\in[L]
we can find
B_{q}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\bar{B}_{r_{*}}(x_{q})\subseteq B_{q}
and such that
r_{*}\leq\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})\leq 2^{-\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{1}{2r_{*}})\rceil+1}\leq 4r_{*}
, which is possible since
r_{*}\leq 1/4
. We then have that for every
q\in[L]
,

\mu(B_{q})\geq\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{*}}(x_{q})\bigr)\geq\xi\cdot r_{*}^{d}\geq\frac{\theta}{\lambda^{d/\beta}}\geq\frac{8}{n}\log(4L/\delta).

Hence

\displaystyle\min_{q\in[L]}

\displaystyle\biggl\{\sup_{x\in B_{q}}\eta(x)-2\lambda\cdot\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B_{q})^{\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log\bigl(2^{2+d}L\cdot n(2+\log_{2}n)/(\alpha\cdot\delta)\bigr)}{n\cdot\mu(B_{q})}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{q\in[L]}\biggl\{\eta(x_{q})-2^{1+2\beta}\cdot\lambda\cdot r_{*}^{\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log\bigl(2^{3+d}3^{d}n^{2}\log_{2}(n)/(\alpha\cdot\delta)\bigr)}{n\cdot\xi\cdot r_{*}^{d}}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\tau+\Delta-2^{3}\cdot\lambda\cdot r_{*}^{\beta}-\sqrt{\frac{\theta}{\xi\cdot r_{*}^{d}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}}}\geq\tau,

so (
S4
) holds. Thus, taking
S^{\dagger}:=\bigcup_{q\in[L]}B_{q}\supseteq\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)
, when
\Delta\leq 1
we may apply Proposition
S2
to see that with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})\geq\sup\big\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}(S^{\dagger})\big\}-2C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\sup\big\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\big\}-2C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq M_{\tau}-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma})-2C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}

(S6)

\displaystyle=M_{\tau}-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(1+2^{5\gamma})\cdot\theta^{\beta\kappa\gamma/\rho}-2C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq M_{\tau}-C\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log_{+}\bigl(n/(\alpha\wedge\delta)\bigr)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}+\biggl(\frac{\log_{+}(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggr\},

(S7)

where
C\geq 1
depends only on
d
,
\kappa
,
\gamma
,
C_{\mathrm{App}}
and
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})
. Finally, if
\Delta>1
, then (
S6
) holds because
\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})\geq 0
,
M_{\tau}\leq 1
and
C_{\mathrm{App}}\geq 1
, so (
S7
) holds too. This completes the proof of the first claim of the proposition.

For the second claim, observe by Proposition
4
that for
\alpha\in(0,1/2]
,

\displaystyle R_{\tau}(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})\leq\frac{M_{\tau}-\mathbb{E}\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})}{\mathbb{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}\subseteq{}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\tau}-\mathbb{E}\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}})}{1-\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log_{+}(n/\alpha)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}+\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\biggr\},

where the final bound follows by integrating the tail bound in the first part of the proposition.
∎

S3
Proofs of claims in Examples

,

and

and a related result

Example
1
:

The marginal density of
X
is convex on
(-\infty,-\nu-2]
and on
[\nu+2,\infty)
, so writing
\phi
for the standard normal density, we have
\omega(x)=\phi(x-\nu)+\phi(x+\nu)
for
|x|\geq\nu+2
. Hence there exists
\xi_{0}\in\bigl(0,1/\sqrt{2\pi}\bigr]
, depending only on
\nu
, such that for
\xi\in(0,\xi_{0}]
we have
\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})=[-x_{\xi},x_{\xi}]
, where
x_{\xi}\in\bigl[\nu+\sqrt{2\log\bigl(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{1/2}\xi}\bigr)},\nu+\sqrt{2\log\bigl(\frac{2^{1/2}}{\pi^{1/2}\xi}\bigr)}\bigr]
satisfies
\phi(x_{\xi}-\nu)+\phi(x_{\xi}+\nu)=\xi
. In fact, when
\nu\geq 2
, we may take
\xi_{0}=2\phi(\nu)
. Moreover,
\eta(x)=\frac{\phi(x-\nu)}{\phi(x-\nu)+\phi(x+\nu)}=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2x\nu}}
, so
\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)=[x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta},\infty)
, where
x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta}:=\frac{1}{2\nu}\log\bigl(\frac{\tau+\Delta}{1-(\tau+\Delta)}\bigr)
. By reducing
\xi_{0}>0
, depending only on
\nu
and
\tau
, if necessary, we may assume that
-x_{\xi}\leq x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta}\leq x_{\xi}
for
\xi\in(0,\xi_{0}]
and
\Delta\in\bigl(0,(1-\tau)/2\bigr]
. Writing
\Phi
for the standard normal distribution function, we deduce that for
\xi\in(0,\xi_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{int}}

\displaystyle\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}=\mu\bigl([x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta},x_{\xi}]\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\xi}-\nu)-\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta}-\nu)+\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\xi}+\nu)-\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta}+\nu).

Using the Mills ratio and the mean value inequality, it follows that for
\xi\in(0,\xi_{0}]
,

\displaystyle M_{\tau}

\displaystyle-\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{int}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\xi}-\nu)-\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\xi}+\nu)-\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\nu,\tau,0}-\nu)-\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\nu,\tau,0}+\nu)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt+\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta}-\nu)+\frac{1}{2}\Phi(x_{\nu,\tau,\Delta}+\nu)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\phi(x_{\xi}-\nu)}{2(x_{\xi}-\nu)}+\frac{\phi(x_{\xi}+\nu)}{2(x_{\xi}+\nu)}+\frac{\Delta}{\sqrt{2\pi}\nu\tau(1-\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi}{2\sqrt{2\log\bigl(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{1/2}\xi_{0}}\bigr)}}+\frac{\Delta}{\sqrt{2\pi}\nu\tau(1-\tau)}.

On the other hand, when
\xi>\xi_{0}
, we have

M_{\tau}-\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{int}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}\leq 1\leq\frac{\xi}{\xi_{0}}.

We conclude that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{int}},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
with
\kappa=\gamma=1
when we take

C_{\mathrm{App}}^{-1}=\min\biggl\{2\sqrt{2\log\biggl(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{1/2}\xi_{0}}\biggr)},\sqrt{2\pi}\nu\tau(1-\tau),\xi_{0}\biggr\}.

Example
2
:

Given
\epsilon_{0}>0
, choose
A_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\cap[0,1]^{d}\bigr)
such that
\mu(A_{0})\geq M_{\tau}-\epsilon_{0}
. Let
\partial A_{0}
and
r=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
denote the boundary and vector of side-lengths of
A_{0}
respectively. Observe that for
\Delta\leq\epsilon\cdot\delta^{1/\gamma}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)

\displaystyle\supseteq\bigl\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\cap[0,1]^{d}:\mathrm{dist}_{\infty}(x,\mathcal{S}_{\tau})\geq(\Delta/\epsilon)^{\gamma}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\bigl\{x\in A_{0}:\mathrm{dist}_{\infty}(x,\partial A_{0})\geq(\Delta/\epsilon)^{\gamma}\bigr\}.

Moreover,
\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})=[0,1]^{d}
for
\xi\leq 1
. For
s>0
, let
A_{0}(s):=\bigl\{x\in A_{0}:\mathrm{dist}_{\infty}(x,\partial A_{0})\geq s\bigr\}
. Note that for
s\leq\min_{j}r_{j}/2
,

\mu(A_{0})-\mu(A_{0}(s))\leq\prod_{j=1}^{d}r_{j}-\prod_{j=1}^{d}(r_{j}-2s)\leq 1-(1-2s)^{d}\leq 2ds.

On the other hand, if
s>\min_{j\in[d]}r_{j}/2
, then

\mu(A_{0})-\mu(A_{0}(s))\leq\prod_{j=1}^{d}r_{j}\leq\min_{j\in[d]}r_{j}<2s.

Then, for
\xi\in[0,1]
and any
\Delta\in(0,\epsilon\cdot\delta^{1/\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle M_{\tau}-\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq M_{\tau}-\mu\bigl(A_{0,(\Delta/\epsilon)^{\gamma}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq M_{\tau}-\mu(A_{0})+2d\Bigl(\frac{\Delta}{\epsilon}\Bigr)^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}+2d\Bigl(\frac{\Delta}{\epsilon}\Bigr)^{\gamma}.

On the other hand, if
\xi>1
or
\Delta>\epsilon\cdot\delta^{1/\gamma}
, then for any
\kappa\in(0,\infty)
and
C_{\mathrm{App}}\geq 1/(\epsilon^{\gamma}\delta)
, we have

M_{\tau}-\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}\leq 1\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma}).

Since
\epsilon_{0}>0
was arbitrary, the conclusion follows.

Example
3
:

Writing
\omega_{\kappa}:=\omega_{\mu_{\kappa},d}
for the lower-density of
\mu_{\kappa}
, we have for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that

\displaystyle\omega_{\kappa}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{t\in[\|x\|_{\infty},\infty)}g_{\kappa}(t)\biggl\{\mathcal{L}_{d}\bigl(\bar{B}_{1\wedge t}(x)\cap\bar{B}_{t}(0)\bigr)\wedge\inf_{r\in(0,1\wedge t)}\frac{\mathcal{L}_{d}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap\bar{B}_{t}(0)\bigr)}{r^{d}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{t\in[\|x\|_{\infty},\infty)}(1\wedge t^{d})g_{\kappa}(t)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}g_{\kappa}(\|x\|_{\infty})\wedge g_{\kappa}(1)&\text{ if }\kappa\in(0,2)\\
\Bigl(\frac{1}{2^{d}(\kappa-1)}\cdot g_{\kappa}(\|x\|_{\infty})\Bigr)\wedge g_{\kappa}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2(\kappa-1)^{1/d}}\Bigr)&\text{ if }\kappa\in[2,\infty).\end{cases}

Now, writing
a_{d,\kappa}:=\frac{1}{2(\kappa-1)^{1/d}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\kappa\geq 2\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\kappa<2\}}
and
\xi_{d,\kappa}:=g_{\kappa}(a_{d,\kappa})
, we have for
\xi\leq\xi_{d,\kappa}
that
\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\kappa})\supseteq\bigl\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|x\|_{\infty}\leq R_{\xi,d,\kappa}\bigr\}
, where

R_{\xi,d,\kappa}:=\begin{cases}\Bigl(\frac{(\kappa/(2^{d}\xi))^{1-\kappa}-1}{1-\kappa}\Bigr)^{1/d}&\text{ if }\kappa\in(0,1)\\
\log^{1/d}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2^{d}\xi}\Bigr)&\text{ if }\kappa=1\\
\Bigl(\frac{1-\{2^{d}\xi/(\kappa a_{d,\kappa}^{d})\}^{\kappa-1}}{\kappa-1}\Bigr)^{1/d}&\text{ if }\kappa\in(1,\infty).\end{cases}

We now calculate that

M_{\tau}\equiv M_{\tau}(P_{\kappa,\gamma},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}})=\mu_{\kappa}\bigl([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\bigr)=1/2.

(S8)

Observe that
\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta_{\gamma})=\{x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:x_{1}\geq(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma}\}
for
\Delta\in(0,1-\tau]
. Hence, for
\Delta\in(0,1-\tau]
and
\xi\leq\xi_{d,\kappa}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup\big\{\mu_{\kappa}(A):A

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\kappa})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta_{\gamma})\bigr)\big\}

\displaystyle\geq\sup\big\{\mu_{\kappa}(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\bar{B}_{R_{\xi,d,\kappa}}(0)\cap\bigl([(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\bigr)\bigr)\big\}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\kappa}\bigl(\bar{B}_{R_{\xi,d,\kappa}}(0)\cap\bigl([(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu_{\kappa}\bigl(\bar{B}_{R_{\xi,d,\kappa}}(0)\bigr)-\mu_{\kappa}\bigl([0,(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\bigr).

(S9)

For
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
x_{-1}:=(x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
. Then

\displaystyle\mu_{\kappa}\bigl([0,(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}g_{\kappa}(\|x\|_{\infty})\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{[0,(\Delta/\lambda)^{\gamma}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}g_{\kappa}(\|x_{-1}\|_{\infty})\,dx=b_{d,\kappa}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\Delta}{\lambda}\biggr)^{\gamma},

(S10)

where

\displaystyle b_{d,\kappa}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}g_{\kappa}(\|x_{-1}\|_{\infty})\,dx_{-1}

\displaystyle=(d-1)\cdot 2^{d-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{d-2}g_{\kappa}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{(1-\kappa)^{1/d}\Gamma(2-1/d)\Gamma(\frac{1+(d-1)\kappa}{d(1-\kappa)})}{2\Gamma(\kappa/(1-\kappa))}&\text{ if }\kappa\in(0,1)\\
\Gamma(2-1/d)/2&\text{ if }\kappa=1\\
\frac{(\kappa-1)^{1/d}\Gamma(2-1/d)\Gamma(2+\frac{1}{\kappa-1})}{2\Gamma(2-\frac{1}{d}+\frac{1}{1-\kappa})}&\text{ if }\kappa\in(1,\infty).\end{cases}

Moreover, for
\xi\leq\xi_{d,\kappa}\leq\kappa/2^{d}
,

1-\mu_{\kappa}\bigl(\bar{B}_{R_{\xi,d,\kappa}}(0)\bigr)=d\cdot 2^{d}\int_{R_{\xi,d,\kappa}}^{\infty}y^{d-1}g_{\kappa}(y)\,dy=\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\xi}{a_{d,\kappa}^{d}\kappa}\biggr)^{\kappa}.

(S11)

But for
\Delta>1-\tau
or
\xi>\xi_{d,\kappa}
, we have

\sup\big\{\mu_{\kappa}(A):A\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\kappa})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta_{\gamma})\bigr)\big\}\geq 0\geq\frac{1}{2}-\biggl(\frac{\Delta}{1-\tau}\biggr)^{\gamma}-\biggl(\frac{\xi}{\xi_{d,\kappa}}\biggr)^{\kappa}.

We deduce from (
S8
), (
S3
), (
S3
) and (
S11
) that
P_{\kappa,\gamma}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
, with

C_{\mathrm{App}}=\biggl(\frac{b_{d,\kappa}^{1/\gamma}}{\lambda}\vee\frac{1}{1-\tau}\biggr)^{\gamma}\vee\biggl(\frac{2^{d}}{a_{d,\kappa}^{d}\kappa}\vee\frac{1}{\xi_{d,\kappa}}\biggr)^{\kappa}.

Given a closed set
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we define the projection
\Pi_{S}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow S
by

\Pi_{S}(x):=\sargmin_{z\in S}\|x-z\|_{2},

where
\sargmin
denotes the smallest element of the
\argmin
in the lexicographic ordering.

Proposition S6
.

Let
P
be a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1]
with marginal
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and continuous regression function
\eta
. Suppose that
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
has the properties that

•

there exists a bounded set
A_{0}\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
such that
\mu(A_{0})=M_{\tau}
;

•

writing
\partial A_{0}
for the topological boundary of
A_{0}
, there exist
s_{0}>0
and
C_{\mathrm{App}}^{\prime}>0
such that for every
s\in(0,s_{0}]
, we can find
A_{0}(s)\in\mathcal{A}
with
A_{0}(s)\subseteq\{x\in A_{0}:\mathrm{dist}_{2}(x,\partial A_{0})>s\}
, satisfying

\mu(A_{0})-\mu\bigl(A_{0}(s)\bigr)\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}^{\prime}\cdot s.

Suppose that there exist
c_{*},\kappa>0
such that

\omega(x)\geq c_{*}\cdot s^{1/\kappa}

for all
x\in A_{0}(s)
and
s\in(0,s_{0})
. Assume further that
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\eta(x)=\tau\}
is non-empty, and that there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that
\eta
is differentiable on
\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\tau}+\delta_{0}B_{2,1}(0)
. Let
A_{0,\delta_{0}}:=A_{0}+\delta_{0}B_{2,1}(0)
and assume that
\epsilon_{0}:=\inf_{x\in A_{0,\delta_{0}}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}}\|\nabla\eta(x)\|_{2}>0
. Then
\Delta_{*}:=\inf_{x\in A_{0}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}}\eta(x)-\tau>0
, and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\tau,\kappa,1,C_{\mathrm{App}})
for

C_{\mathrm{App}}\geq\max\biggl\{\frac{C_{\mathrm{App}}^{\prime}}{\max(c_{*}^{\kappa},\epsilon_{0})},\frac{1}{s_{0}c_{*}^{\kappa}},\frac{1}{\min(\epsilon_{0}\delta_{0},\Delta_{*})}\biggr\}.

Since
\eta
is continuous on the intersection of the closure of
A_{0}
with the complement of
\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}
, and since this intersection is compact but does not contain any point in
\mathcal{S}_{\tau}
, we have that
\Delta_{*}>0
. By
Cannings, Berrett and
Samworth 2020
, we have

\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}=\biggl\{x_{0}+\frac{t\nabla\eta(x_{0})}{\|\nabla\eta(x_{0})\|_{2}}:x_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{\tau},|t|<\delta_{0}\biggr\}.

(S12)

Moreover, from the proof of that result, we see that for any
x\in\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{1}}
, we can take
x_{0}=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}}(x)
in the representation (
S12
).
Now suppose that
x\in A_{0}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}
, so that
x=x_{0}+t\nabla\eta(x_{0})/\|\nabla\eta(x_{0})\|_{2}
with
x_{0}=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}_{\tau}}(x)\in A_{0,\delta_{0}}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau}
and
|t|=\mathrm{dist}_{2}(x,\mathcal{S}_{\tau})<\delta_{0}
. Since the line segment joining
x_{0}
and
x
is contained in
A_{0,\delta_{0}}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\delta_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle|\eta(x)-\tau|=\biggl|\eta\biggl(x_{0}+\frac{t\nabla\eta(x_{0})}{\|\nabla\eta(x_{0})\|_{2}}\biggr)-\eta(x_{0})\biggr|\geq|t|\epsilon_{0}.

(S13)

Now observe that
\mathrm{int}(A_{0})\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau}=\emptyset
because if
x_{0}\in\mathrm{int}(A_{0})\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau}
, then for sufficiently small
t>0
, the point
x_{0}-t\nabla\eta(x_{0})/\|\nabla\eta(x_{0})\|_{2}
would belong to
A_{0}
and
\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)^{\mathrm{c}}
, a contradiction. Hence, for
x\in A_{0}
we have
\mathrm{dist}_{2}(x,\mathcal{S}_{\tau})\geq\mathrm{dist}_{2}(x,\partial A_{0})
, so for
\Delta<\min(\epsilon_{0}\delta_{0},\Delta_{*})
,

\displaystyle\{x\in A_{0}:\eta(x)\in[\tau,\tau+\Delta)\}\subseteq A_{0}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\tau,\Delta/\epsilon_{0}}\subseteq\bigl\{x\in A_{0}:\mathrm{dist}_{2}(x,\partial A_{0})\leq\Delta/\epsilon_{0}\bigr\}.

Then, for any
\xi\in\bigl(0,c_{*}s_{0}^{1/\kappa}\bigr)
and
\Delta\in\bigl(0,\min(\epsilon_{0}\delta_{0},\Delta_{*})\bigr)
,

\displaystyle M_{\tau}-\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})

\displaystyle\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}\leq\mu(A_{0})-\mu\biggl(A_{0}\Bigl(\frac{\xi^{\kappa}}{c_{*}^{\kappa}}\wedge\frac{\Delta}{\epsilon_{0}}\Bigr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}^{\prime}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\xi^{\kappa}}{c_{*}^{\kappa}}\wedge\frac{\Delta}{\epsilon_{0}}\biggr)\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta).

On the other hand, if
\xi\geq c_{*}s_{0}^{1/\kappa}
or
\Delta\geq\min(\epsilon_{0}\delta_{0},\Delta_{*})
, then provided we take
C_{\mathrm{App}}\geq\max\bigl\{1/(s_{0}c_{*}^{\kappa}),1/\min(\epsilon_{0}\delta_{0},\Delta_{*})\bigr\}
, we have

\displaystyle M_{\tau}-\sup\bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}\leq 1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s_{0}}\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{c_{*}}\Bigr)^{\kappa}+\frac{\Delta}{\min(\epsilon_{0}\delta_{0},\Delta_{*})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta).

The result follows.
∎

S4
Proofs from Section
3

S4.1
Proofs from Section
3.1

In order to prove Theorem
6
, we first establish Proposition
S7
below, which will be useful in the sequel. Recall the definition of
\Delta_{n,\beta,\lambda}
from (
10
).

Proposition S7
.

Given
\delta\in(0,1)
and
x_{0}
,
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we let

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1}):=\bigcap\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|-\Delta_{n,\beta,\lambda}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n,\beta,\lambda}(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}\biggr)\leq\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\delta}\leq\lambda\biggr\},

where the intersection is over all
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,1]\times[1,\infty)
for which
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\tau)
and
\min_{x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}}\{\eta(x)-\Delta_{n,\beta,\lambda}(x)\}\geq\tau
. Here we adopt the convention that an intersection over an empty set is the entire probability space. Then
\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})\bigr)\geq 1-\delta
.

The proof of Proposition
S7
will make use of the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}:=\bigcap_{(i,k)\in[n]^{2}}\biggl\{\biggl|\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\bigr)-\frac{\sum_{t\in[n]}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}}{\sum_{t\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}}\biggr|\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{2k}}\biggr\}.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, and
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We will assume without loss of generality that
\eta(x_{0})\geq\eta(x_{1})
and
\min\{\omega(x_{0}),\omega(x_{1})\}>0
since otherwise the index set in the intersection in the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
is empty. By Hoeffding’s lemma and a union bound, we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}^{\mathrm{c}}|\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigr)\leq\delta/2
. For
\ell\in[n]
and
x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
we let
s_{\ell}(x):=\bigl(n\omega(x)/(2\ell)\bigr)^{-1/d}
and define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}}\bigcap_{\ell=\lceil 4\log(4n/\delta)\rceil}^{\lfloor n\omega(x)/2\rfloor}\bigl\{|\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\cap\bar{B}_{s_{\ell}(x)}(x)|\geq\ell\bigr\}.

Observe that for
x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
and
\ell\in\{\lceil 4\log(4n/\delta)\rceil,\ldots,\lfloor n\omega(x)/2\rfloor\}
we have
s_{\ell}(x)\in(0,1]
and

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{s_{\ell}(x)}(x)\bigr)\geq\omega(x)\cdot s_{\ell}(x)^{d}=\frac{2\ell}{n}.

(S14)

Hence, by the multiplicative Chernoff bound (Lemma
S39
) we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})^{\mathrm{c}}\bigr)\leq\delta/2
. Consequently, to complete the proof it suffices to show that
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}_{n,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
. To this end, fix
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,1]\times[1,\infty)
for which
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\tau)
and
\eta(x)-\Delta_{n}(x)\geq\tau
for
x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
. On the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}
, we have for any
(i,k)\in[n]^{2}
with
\hat{\psi}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,k)>\lambda
that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t\in[n]}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}}{\sum_{t\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}}\geq\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\bigr)-\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{2k}}>\tau+\lambda\cdot(2r_{i,k})^{\beta}.

Hence,
\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
, since
\eta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta))
. Consequently, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}
, given any
(i,j,k,\ell)\in[n]^{4}
with
\min\bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,k),\hat{\psi}_{n,\beta,\delta}(j,\ell)\bigr\}>\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\bigr)

\displaystyle-\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{j,\ell}}(X_{j})\bigr)-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{k\wedge\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{t\in[n]}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}{\sum_{t\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}-\frac{\sum_{t\in[n]}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{j,\ell}}(X_{j})\}}}{\sum_{t\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{j,\ell}}(X_{j})\}}}

\displaystyle\leq\eta(X_{i})+\lambda\cdot r_{i,k}^{\beta}-\eta(X_{j})+\lambda\cdot r_{j,\ell}^{\beta}\leq\lambda\cdot\bigl(\|X_{i}-X_{j}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}+r_{i,k}^{\beta}+r_{j,\ell}^{\beta}\bigr),

and hence
\hat{\phi}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,j,k,\ell)\leq\lambda
. Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}
, we have
\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\delta}\leq\lambda
.

For the lower bound, assume without loss of generality that
\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})\leq 1
and let
i_{0}:=\sargmin_{i\in[n]}\|X_{i}-x_{0}\|_{\infty}
and
i_{1}:=\sargmin_{i\in[n]}\|X_{i}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}
. For
x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
, let
\rho_{n}(x):=\bigl\{\Delta_{n}(x)/(24\lambda)\bigr\}^{1/\beta}
and
k_{n}(x):=\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\cdot\omega(x)\cdot\rho_{n}(x)^{d}\rfloor
. Since for each
x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
, the fact that
\Delta_{n}(x)\leq 1
ensures that
\frac{n}{2}\cdot\omega(x)\cdot\rho_{n}(x)^{d}\geq 4\log(4n/\delta)+2
, we have
k_{n}(x)\in\{\lceil 4\log(4n/\delta)\rceil,\ldots,n\}
. Hence, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
we have
|\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\cap\bar{B}_{\rho_{n}(x)}(x)|\geq k_{n}(x)\bigr\}
for
x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
.

Now fix
j\in\{0,1\}
, so that
\|X_{i_{j}}-x_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\rho_{n}(x_{j})
and
r_{i_{j},k_{n}(x_{j})}\leq 2\cdot\rho_{n}(x_{j})
. Then
\eta(x_{j})\geq\tau+\Delta_{n}(x_{j})=\tau+24\lambda\rho_{n}(x_{j})^{\beta}
and
\eta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta))
, so
\bar{B}_{r_{i_{j},k_{n}(x_{j})}}(X_{i_{j}})\subseteq\bar{B}_{3\cdot\rho_{n}(x_{j})}(x_{j})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
. Since
\Delta_{n}(x_{j})\leq 1
, we also have
\sqrt{2\log(4n^{2}/\delta)/k_{n}(x_{j})}\leq\Delta_{n}(x_{j})/4
. Hence, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{i_{j},k_{n}(x_{j})}}(X_{i_{j}})\bigr)

\displaystyle-\tau-\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{2k_{n}(x_{j})}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{t\in[n]}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i_{j},k_{n}(x_{j})}}(X_{i_{j}})\}}}{\sum_{t\in[n]}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i_{j}},k_{n}(x_{j})}(X_{i_{j}})}\}}-\tau-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{k_{n}(x_{j})}}

\displaystyle\geq\eta(x_{j})-\lambda\cdot\{3\cdot\rho_{n}(x_{j})\}^{\beta}-\tau-\sqrt{\frac{2\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{k_{n}(x_{j})}}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta_{n}(x_{j})-\frac{3^{\beta}}{24}\cdot\Delta_{n}(x_{j})-\frac{1}{4}\cdot\Delta_{n}(x_{j})\geq\frac{5}{8}\cdot\Delta_{n}(x_{j})

\displaystyle=15\lambda\cdot\rho_{n}(x_{j})^{\beta}>\lambda\cdot(2r_{i_{j},k_{n}(x_{j})})^{\beta}.

Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
, we have
\hat{\psi}_{n,\beta,\delta}\bigl(i_{j},k_{n}(x_{j})\bigr)>\lambda
. Hence, whenever
\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})\leq 1
, we have on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
that

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{n,\beta,\delta}\bigl(i_{0},i_{1},k_{n}(x_{0}),k_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{i_{0},k_{n}(x_{0})}}(X_{i_{0}})\bigr)-\hat{\eta}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r_{i_{1},k_{n}(x_{1})}}(X_{i_{1}})\bigr)-\sqrt{2\log(4n^{2}/\delta)/\{k_{n}(x_{0})\wedge k_{n}(x_{1})\}}}{\|X_{i_{0}}-X_{i_{1}}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}+r_{i_{0},k_{n}(x_{0})}^{\beta}+r_{i_{1},k_{n}(x_{1})}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-2\lambda\bigl\{3\bigl(\rho_{n}(x_{0})\vee\rho_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)\bigr\}^{\beta}-2\sqrt{2\log(4n^{2}/\delta)/\{k_{n}(x_{0})\wedge k_{1}(x_{0})\}}}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}+3\cdot\bigl\{2\cdot\bigl(\rho_{n}(x_{0})\vee\rho_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)\bigr\}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-\frac{3}{4}\bigl(\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}+\frac{1}{4\lambda}\bigl(\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-\frac{3}{4}\bigl(\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}-\frac{\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})}{4\lambda}\cdot\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2\beta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}.

Thus, whenever
\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})\leq 1
, we have on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\delta}(x_{0},x_{1})
that

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\delta}

\displaystyle\geq\min\bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n,\beta,\delta}\bigl(i_{0},k_{n}(x_{0})\bigr),\hat{\psi}_{n,\beta,\delta}\bigl(i_{1},k_{n}(x_{1})\bigr),\hat{\phi}_{n,\beta,\delta}\bigl(i_{0},i_{1},k_{n}(x_{0}),k_{n}(x_{1})\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\min\biggl\{\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}},\lambda\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\min\biggl\{\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}},\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})-\Delta_{n}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n}(x_{1})}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}},

as required.
∎

Theorem
6
is an immediate consequence of Proposition
S7
combined with the continuity of probability from below.
∎

The following four lemmas are used in the proof of Corollary
7
.

Lemma S8
.

Let
\mu
be a Borel probability distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with lower density
\omega
. Then
\mu\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega(x)=0\}\bigr)=0
. Hence
\mathrm{supp}(\mu)=\mathrm{cl}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega(x)>0\}\bigr)
.

Let
Z:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega(x)=0\}
and fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. By the monotone convergence theorem we may choose
R>0
sufficiently large that
\mu\bigl(Z\setminus\bar{B}_{R}(0)\bigr)<\epsilon/2
and let
\xi:=\epsilon/\bigl(2\cdot\{5(R+1)\}^{d}\bigr)
. By
Reeve, Cannings and
Samworth 2021
,

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(Z\cap\bar{B}_{R}(0)\bigr)\leq\mu\bigl(\bigl\{x\in\bar{B}_{R}(0):\omega(x)<\xi\bigr\}\bigr)\leq\{5(R+1)\}^{d}\cdot\xi=\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Hence
\mu(Z)=0
, so
\mu\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega(x)>0\}\bigr)=1
, and consequently
\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\subseteq\mathrm{cl}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega(x)>0\}\bigr)
. Moreover, if
z\in\mathrm{cl}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\omega(x)>0\}\bigr)
then every neighbourhood of
z
has positive
\mu
-measure, so
z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
. The second conclusion therefore follows.
∎Recall the definitions of
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)
from Section
3.1
.

Lemma S9
.

Given any distribution
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)
, we have
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)={\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)
where

{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P):=\sup\biggl\{\frac{|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}:x_{0}\neq x_{1},\eta(x_{0})\wedge\eta(x_{1})>\tau,\omega(x_{0})\wedge\omega(x_{1})>0\biggr\}\vee 1.

First note that if
\eta(x)>\tau
then
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
and if
\omega(x)>0
then
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, so
{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)
.

Since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)
, it has a continuous regression function
\eta
such that
\eta^{-1}([\tau,\tau+\varepsilon))\subseteq\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
for some
\varepsilon>0
. Now take distinct points
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
, and suppose initially that
\eta(x_{0})\wedge\eta(x_{1})>\tau
. Given
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, it follows from Lemma
S8
that we may choose
x_{0}^{\prime},x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\eta(x_{0}^{\prime})\wedge\eta(x_{1}^{\prime})>\tau
and
\omega(x_{0}^{\prime})\wedge\omega(x_{1}^{\prime})>0
such that

\displaystyle\max\bigl\{\|x_{0}-x_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty},\|x_{1}-x_{1}^{\prime}\|_{\infty},|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{0}^{\prime})|,|\eta(x_{1})-\eta(x_{1}^{\prime})|\bigr\}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\leq|\eta(x_{0}^{\prime})-\eta(x_{1}^{\prime})|+2\epsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{1}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}+2\epsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\bigl(\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}+2\epsilon^{\prime}\bigr)^{\beta}+2\epsilon^{\prime}.

(S15)

Since
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
was arbitrary, we deduce that
|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
for all distinct
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\eta^{-1}\bigl((\tau,1]\bigr)
. Now consider
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
with
\eta(x_{0})=\tau
and
\eta(x_{1})>\tau
. Given
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\bigl(0,\min\{\varepsilon,\eta(x_{1})-\tau\}\bigr)
, by the intermediate value theorem we may choose
x_{2}
on the line segment between
x_{0}
and
x_{1}
with
\eta(x_{2})=\tau+\epsilon^{\prime}
. It then follows that
x_{2}\in\eta^{-1}\bigl([\tau,\tau+\varepsilon)\bigr)\subseteq\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
. By the consequence of (
S4.1
), we deduce that
|\eta(x_{2})-\eta(x_{1})|\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
, and hence

\displaystyle|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{2})|+|\eta(x_{2})-\eta(x_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\prime}+{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}+{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}.

Again, since
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\bigl(0,\min\{\varepsilon,\eta(x_{1})-\tau\}\bigr)
can be taken arbitrarily small, we deduce that
|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
for all
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
with
\eta(x_{1})>\tau
. Finally, when
\eta(x_{0})=\eta(x_{1})=\tau
, the inequality
|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
is immediate. Thus, we have
|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
for all
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, so
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\leq{\lambda_{\beta,\flat}}(P)
, as required.
∎Our next lemma adapts ideas from
McShane 1934
.

Lemma S10
.

Given
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)
with
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)<\infty
, we have
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}\bigl(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P),\tau\bigr)
.

Since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)
, it has a continuous regression function
\eta_{0}
such that
\eta_{0}^{-1}\bigl([\tau,\tau+\varepsilon)\bigr)\subseteq\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
for some
\varepsilon>0
. We construct a regression function
\eta_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
by

\displaystyle\eta_{1}(x):=\begin{cases}1\wedge\inf\bigl\{\eta_{0}(z)+\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|z-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta}:z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})\bigr\}&\text{ if }x\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})\\
\eta_{0}(x)&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

First note that
\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})=\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{1})
. We claim that
|\eta_{1}(x)-\eta_{1}(x^{\prime})|\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
for all
x
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
. Indeed, if
\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})=\emptyset
, then
\eta_{1}(x)=\eta_{1}(x^{\prime})
, and if
z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
, then

\displaystyle\eta_{0}(z)+\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|z-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta}\leq\eta_{0}(z)+\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|z-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}+\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta},

and taking an infimum over
z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
yields
\eta_{1}(x)-\eta_{1}(x^{\prime})\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
. By interchanging the roles of
x
and
x^{\prime}
, the claim follows. Our second claim is that
\eta_{1}(x)=\eta_{0}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
. To see this, first observe that
\eta_{1}(x)\leq\eta_{0}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
by definition of
\eta_{1}
. On the other hand, by definition of
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)
, we have for any
z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
that

\eta_{0}(x)\leq\eta_{0}(z)+\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|z-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta},

so taking an infimum over
z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
yields
\eta_{0}(x)\leq\eta_{1}(x)
. In particular, it now follows that
\eta_{0}(x)=\eta_{1}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
. To show that
\eta_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}\bigl(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P),\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{1})\bigr)
, we must also verify that
\eta_{1}
is continuous. To this end, let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. If
\eta_{0}(x)>\tau
, then since
\eta_{0}
is continuous, we have for all sufficiently small
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
and
z\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)
that
\eta_{0}(z)>\tau
. Hence
|\eta_{1}(x)-\eta_{1}(z)|\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|x-z\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
by our first claim, so
\eta_{1}
is continuous on
\eta_{0}^{-1}\bigl((\tau,1]\bigr)
. On the other hand, if
\eta_{0}(x)<\tau+\varepsilon
then, again, since
\eta_{0}
is continuous, for all sufficiently small
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
and
z\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)
we also have have
\eta_{0}(z)<\tau+\varepsilon
, so
x,z\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{0})
. We deduce from our second claim that
\eta_{1}(x)=\eta_{0}(x)
and
\eta_{1}(z)=\eta_{0}(z)
, so again,
\eta_{1}
is continuous at
x
. We conclude that
\eta_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}\bigl(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P),\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{1})\bigr)
. Moreover, since
\eta_{0}
is a regression function for
P
, and
\eta_{0}(x)=\eta_{1}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, we have that
\eta_{1}
is also a regression function for
P
, and hence
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}\bigl(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P),\tau\bigr)
.
∎

Recall the definition of the classes
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})
from Section
3.1
.

Lemma S11
.

Fix
\beta\in(0,1]
and
\lambda\geq 1
. Then

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\tau)\subseteq\bigcup_{\underline{\epsilon}\in(0,\infty)^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon}).

Let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\tau)
. Then
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\leq\lambda<\infty
. Moreover, since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)
, we have by Lemma
S9
that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})
for sufficiently small
\underline{\epsilon}=(\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\in(0,1]^{3}
.
∎Finally, we are in a position to prove Corollary
7
.

First, since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})
we may choose
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\epsilon_{0}}(\eta)
with
\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}\geq\epsilon_{1}
, as well as
\min\{\omega(x_{0}),\omega(x_{1})\}\geq\epsilon_{2}
and

\displaystyle|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\geq\frac{3}{4}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)>1\}}.

Writing
\lambda=\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)
, we have by Lemma
S10
that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\tau)
. Define

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle:=192\cdot\lambda^{d/(2\beta+d)}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\log(2n/\delta)}{n\{\omega(x_{0})\wedge\omega(x_{1})\}}\biggr)^{\beta/(2\beta+d)}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}\leq 192\cdot\lambda^{d/(2\beta+d)}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\log(2n/\delta)}{n\cdot\epsilon_{2}}\biggr)^{\beta/(2\beta+d)}.

Observe that when
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta\in(0,1)
satisfy (
11
), we have
\Delta\leq\min\{\epsilon_{0},\lambda\epsilon_{1}^{\beta}/4\}
. By Theorem
6
we have
\mathbb{P}_{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle:=\biggl\{\frac{|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|-\Delta}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\delta}\leq\lambda\biggr\}.

Note that on the event
\mathcal{E}
, we have
2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\delta}\leq 2\lambda
, and if
\lambda=1
, then
\lambda\leq 2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\delta}
is immediate. On the other hand, if
\lambda>1
then on the event
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|-\Delta}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\cdot\lambda-\frac{\lambda\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{\beta}}{4\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}}\geq\frac{\lambda}{2},

as required.
∎

First, for
\lambda^{\prime}\in[1,\infty)
, let
S_{\lambda^{\prime}}
denote the union
\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha_{n}}]}B_{(\ell)}
appearing in line 6 of Algorithm
1
when it is applied with
\alpha_{n}=(\alpha/2)\wedge(1/n)
in place of
\alpha
and
\lambda^{\prime}
in place of
\lambda
, and let
\hat{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
denote the corresponding output set in
\mathcal{A}
. Observe that if
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\leq\lambda_{1}^{\prime}
then
S_{\lambda^{\prime}_{1}}\subseteq S_{\lambda^{\prime}_{0}}
, since the
p
-values in (
9
) satisfy
\hat{p}_{n,\beta,\lambda_{0}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\leq\hat{p}_{n,\beta,\lambda_{1}^{\prime}}(\cdot)
. Consequently,
\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{1}})\leq\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{0}})
by line 6 in Algorithm
1
.

(i)
Let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})
. By Lemma
S10
we have
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}\bigl(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P),\tau\bigr)
. Hence, by Lemma
S1
and Corollary
7
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{D})\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\alpha_{n}}}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\alpha_{n}}}\nsubseteq S_{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\alpha_{n}}<\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\bigr)\leq 2\alpha_{n}\leq\alpha.

(S16)

(ii)
Now suppose that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
. On the event
\{2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\alpha_{n}}\leq 2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\}
, we have
\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime})=\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\beta,\alpha_{n}}})\geq\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})
. Hence, by Lemma
S36
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\mu(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})-\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\mu(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})-\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})-\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime})+\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime})-\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\mathrm{VC}}~\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}=:\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}.

(S17)

By Lemmas
S10
and
S1
we have
\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\leq\alpha_{n}
. It follows by (
S4.1
) and (
S4.1
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}

\displaystyle\bigl\{\bigl(M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime})\bigr)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}}\bigr\}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big\{M_{\tau}-\mu\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime}\bigr)\big\}+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{D})\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big\{M_{\tau}-\mu\bigl(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\bigr)\big\}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}+2\alpha_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})\bigr)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}}\bigr\}+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}+2\alpha_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})\bigm|\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+3\alpha_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}

\displaystyle=R_{\tau}(\hat{A}_{2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)})+3\alpha_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\biggl\{\biggl({\frac{\bigl(2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\bigr)^{d/\beta}}{n}\cdot\log_{+}\Bigl(\frac{n}{(\alpha/2)\wedge(1/n)}\Bigr)\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}}+\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\biggr\}+3\alpha_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n},

where we have used Proposition
5
with
C^{\prime}
in place of
C
for the final inequality. The regret bound on
\hat{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{OSS}}
follows by once again using
\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\geq 1-\alpha_{n}\geq 1/2
.
∎

S4.2
Proofs from Section
3.2

Recall the definition of the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}
from Section
S4.1
.

Lemma S12
.

Let
P
be a distribution with regression function
\eta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\mathbb{R}^{d})
. On the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}
, we have
v\geq\beta\cdot u-\log\lambda
for all
(u,v)\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
.

If
(u,v)\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
, then we can find
(i,j,k,\ell)\in[n]^{4}
with
\hat{\varphi}^{\dagger}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,j,k,\ell)<\infty
such that
u=\hat{\varepsilon}^{\dagger}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,j,k,\ell)
and
v=\hat{\varphi}^{\dagger}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,j,k,\ell)
. Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\geq-\log\Biggl(\Biggl|\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{n}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}}{\sum_{t=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\}}}}-\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{n}\eta(X_{t})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{j,\ell}}(X_{j})\}}}}{\sum_{t=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{{\{X_{t}\in\bar{B}_{r_{j,\ell}}(X_{j})\}}}}\Biggr|\Biggr)

\displaystyle\geq-\log\bigl(\lambda(\|X_{i}-X_{j}\|_{\infty}+r_{i,k}+r_{j,\ell})^{\beta}\bigr)=\beta\cdot u-\log\lambda,

where we have applied
\eta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\mathbb{R}^{d})
in the final inequality.
∎Now for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
s>0
we define the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mu}(x,s):=\bigl\{\hat{\mu}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{s}(x)\bigr)\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{s}(x)\bigr)\bigr\}
.

Lemma S13
.

Let
P
be a distribution with regression function
\eta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda,\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Suppose that
x_{0}
,
x_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{\circ}(\mu,c_{0})
and
s\in(0,1]
satisfy

\displaystyle|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\geq 4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{c_{0}ns^{d}}}+6\lambda s^{\beta}.

Then, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu}(x_{0},s)\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu}(x_{1},s)
, there exists
(u,v)\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
with

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\geq-\log\bigl(\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}+6s\bigr)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\leq-\log\biggl(|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|-4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{c_{0}ns^{d}}}-6\lambda s^{\beta}\biggr).

Suppose that the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu}(x_{0},s)\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mu}(x_{1},s)
holds. Then in particular,

\displaystyle\min\bigl\{\hat{\mu}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{s}(x_{0})\bigr),\hat{\mu}_{n}\bigl(\bar{B}_{s}(x_{1})\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot c_{0}\cdot s^{d}>0.

(S18)

As such, we may choose
X_{i}\in\bar{B}_{s}(x_{0})
and
X_{j}\in\bar{B}_{s}(x_{1})
. Moreover, letting
k=\ell=\lceil\frac{n}{2}\cdot c_{0}\cdot s^{d}\rceil
, it follows from (
S18
) that
\max(r_{i,k},r_{j,\ell})\leq 2s
. Now take
u=\hat{\varepsilon}^{\dagger}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,j,k,\ell)
and
v=\hat{\varphi}^{\dagger}_{n,\beta,\delta}(i,j,k,\ell)
so that
(u,v)\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
. The lower bound on
u
follows. The upper bound on
v
then follows from our event combined with the facts that
\bar{B}_{r_{i,k}}(X_{i})\subseteq\bar{B}_{3s}(x_{0})
and
\bar{B}_{r_{j,k}}(X_{j})\subseteq\bar{B}_{3s}(x_{1})
.
∎

Lemma S14
.

Let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{\dagger}(\beta,\lambda,\lambda_{0},c_{0},r_{0})
with
\lambda_{0}\leq\lambda
and
0<r\leq r^{\prime}\leq r_{0}\leq 1
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r,r^{\prime})
denote the event that there exist
(u,v),(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
with
u\geq-\log(7r)
,
u^{\prime}\geq-\log(7r^{\prime})
,
v\leq-\log\bigl((1/2)\lambda_{0}r^{\beta}\bigr)
and
v^{\prime}\leq-\log\bigl((1/2)\lambda_{0}(r^{\prime})^{\beta}\bigr)
. Then provided that

\displaystyle\frac{n}{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}\geq\frac{\bigl(16\lambda/\lambda_{0}\bigr)^{d/\beta}}{c_{0}}\cdot\max\biggl\{\frac{2^{10}}{\lambda_{0}^{2}\cdot r^{2\beta+d}},\frac{8}{r^{d}}\biggr\},

(S19)

we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r,r^{\prime})\bigr)\geq 1-\delta
.

Since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{\dagger}(\beta,\lambda,\lambda_{0},c_{0},r_{0})
, there exist
x_{0},x_{1},x_{0}^{\prime},x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{\circ}(\mu,c_{0})
with
\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq r
,
\|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{1}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq r^{\prime}
,
|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\geq\lambda_{0}\cdot r^{\beta}
and
|\eta(x_{0}^{\prime})-\eta(x_{1}^{\prime})|\geq\lambda_{0}\cdot r^{\prime\beta}
. Now let
s:=\{\lambda_{0}/(16\lambda)\}^{1/\beta}\cdot r\in(0,1]
and introduce the event

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\delta}(r,r^{\prime}):=\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\bigcap_{z\in\{x_{0},x_{1},x_{0}^{\prime},x_{1}^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{E}_{\mu}(z,s).

When (
S19
) holds, we have

\displaystyle 4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{c_{0}ns^{d}}}+6\lambda s^{\beta}

\displaystyle=4\sqrt{\frac{(16\lambda/\lambda_{0})^{d/\beta}\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{c_{0}nr^{d}}}+\frac{3}{8}\cdot\lambda_{0}r^{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\lambda_{0}r^{\beta}.

Hence, by Lemma
S13
, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\delta}(r,r^{\prime})
and when (
S19
) holds, there exist pairs
(u,v),(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
with
u\geq-\log(7r)
,
u^{\prime}\geq-\log(7r^{\prime})
,
v\leq-\log\bigl((1/2)\lambda_{0}r^{\beta}\bigr)
and
v^{\prime}\leq-\log\bigl((1/2)\lambda_{0}(r^{\prime})^{\beta}\bigr)
. Thus, when (
S19
) holds, we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r,r^{\prime})\bigr)\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\delta}(r,r^{\prime})\bigr)\geq 1-\delta

by Hoeffding’s inequality (Lemma
S38
) and the multiplicative Chernoff bound (Lemma
S39
).
∎

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
9
.

First, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying (
13
), let

\displaystyle r_{n}:=\biggl\{\frac{2^{10}\bigl(16\lambda/\lambda_{0}\bigr)^{d/\beta}}{c_{0}\cdot\lambda_{0}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{n}\biggr\}^{1/(2\beta+d)}.

and
r_{n}^{\prime}:=n^{-1/(7+2d)}
. The sample size condition (
13
) ensures that (
S19
) holds and that
r_{n}\leq r_{n}^{\prime}\leq r_{0}
, so we may apply Lemma
S14
to obtain
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime})\bigr)\geq 1-\delta
.
