Next, we show that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime})
we have
\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\leq\beta
. Indeed, suppose that
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime})\cap\{\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}>0\}
holds and let
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
be such that
u_{0}\leq\frac{\log n}{6+2d}
. By Lemma
S12
we have
v_{0}\geq\beta\cdot u_{0}-\log\lambda
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime})
holds, there exists
(u_{1},v_{1})\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
with
u_{1}\geq-\log(7r_{n})
and
v_{1}\leq-\log\bigl((1/2)\lambda_{0}r_{n}^{\beta}\bigr)\leq\beta u_{1}+\log(14/\lambda_{0})
. Moreover, by (
13
) we have
u_{1}\geq\frac{\log n}{3+d}\geq 2u_{0}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\leq\frac{v_{1}-v_{0}-\log f(n)}{u_{1}-u_{0}}\leq\beta+\frac{\log(14\lambda/\lambda_{0})-\log f(n)}{u_{1}-u_{0}}\leq\beta,

where we have again applied (
13
) to ensure that
f(n)\geq 14\lambda/\lambda_{0}
.

Finally, we show that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\eta,\delta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime})
we have
\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\geq\beta-\frac{2(7+2d)\log f(n)}{\log n}
. Indeed, on
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\dagger}(r_{n},r_{n}^{\prime})\cap\{\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}<\beta\}
, there exists
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
with
u_{0}\geq-\log(7r_{n}^{\prime})
and
v_{0}\leq-\log\bigl((1/2)\lambda_{0}(r^{\prime}_{n})^{\beta}\bigr)\leq\beta\cdot u_{0}+\log\bigl(14/\lambda_{0}\bigr)
. Now fix any
(u_{1},v_{1})\in\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\delta}^{\dagger}
satisfying
u_{1}\geq 2u_{0}
. By
Lemma
S12
we have
v_{1}\geq\beta\cdot u_{1}-\log\lambda
and consequently,

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\geq\frac{v_{1}-v_{0}-\log f(n)}{u_{1}-u_{0}}\geq\beta-\frac{\log(14\lambda/\lambda_{0})+\log f(n)}{u_{1}-u_{0}}\geq\beta-\frac{2(7+2d)\log f(n)}{\log n},

as required.
∎

The following lemma is analogous to Corollary
7
but with an estimated Hölder exponent.

Lemma S15
.

Fix
\beta\in(0,1]
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
\lambda\in[1,\infty)
,
\lambda_{0}\in(0,\lambda]
,
c_{0},r_{0}\in(0,1]
and
\bm{\epsilon}=(\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\in(0,1]^{3}
and take
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{\dagger}(\beta,\lambda,\lambda_{0},c_{0},r_{0})
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta\in(0,1)
be such that
14\lambda/\lambda_{0}\leq f(n)\leq n^{\frac{\log(9/7)}{2(7+2d)\log(1/\epsilon_{2})}}
the sample size condition
(
13
) holds and

\frac{n}{\log(2n/\delta)}\geq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{2}}\cdot\biggl\{f(n)^{2(7+2d)}\cdot\Bigl(192\cdot\max\Bigl\{\frac{\lambda}{\epsilon_{0}},\frac{12}{\epsilon_{1}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{2+d}\biggr\}^{1/\beta}.

(S20)

Then

\mathbb{P}_{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\beta-\frac{2(7+2d)\log f(n)}{\log n}\leq\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\leq\beta\biggr\}\cap\biggl\{\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)}{2}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\delta}\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)\biggr\}\biggr)\geq 1-2\delta.

For brevity we write
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}=\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}(P)
.
Since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})
, we may choose
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\epsilon_{0}}(\eta)
with
\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}\geq\epsilon_{1}
, as well as
\min\{\omega(x_{0}),\omega(x_{1})\}\geq\epsilon_{2}
and

\displaystyle|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|\geq\frac{3}{4}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\cdot\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}>1\}}.

By Lemma
S10
, we have
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\tau)
. Hence, on the event
\{\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\leq\beta\}
we have
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\tau)
. Letting
\beta_{n}^{\flat}:=\max\{\beta-2(7+2d)\log f(n)/\log n,0\}
, it follows from Theorem
9
, combined with the sample size condition (
13
) and the lower bound on
f(n)
, that
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\beta_{n}^{\flat}\leq\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\leq\beta\bigr)\geq 1-\delta
. In addition, define
\hat{\Delta}_{n}:=\Delta_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}(x_{0})\vee\Delta_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}(x_{1})
, so that provided
\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\geq\beta_{n}^{\flat}
we have

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}_{n}

\displaystyle=192\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}^{d/(2\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}+d)}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\log(2n/\delta)}{n\{\omega(x_{0})\wedge\omega(x_{1})\}}\biggr)^{\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}/(2\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}+d)}

\displaystyle\leq 192\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\log(2n/\delta)}{n\cdot\epsilon_{2}}\biggr)^{\beta_{n}^{\flat}/(2+d)}\leq\min\Bigl\{\epsilon_{0},\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\epsilon_{1}}{12}\Bigr\},

where we have applied the sample size condition (
S20
) in both inequalities. In particular, we have
\min_{x\in\{x_{0},x_{1}\}}\{\eta(x)-\Delta_{n,\beta,\lambda}(x)\}\geq\tau
on the event
\bigl\{\beta_{n}^{\flat}\leq\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\bigr\}
and under our sample size conditions. Consequently, if we let
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\delta}
denote the event

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\delta}

\displaystyle:=\bigl\{\beta_{n}^{\flat}\leq\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}\leq\beta\bigr\}\cap\biggl\{\frac{|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|-\hat{\Delta}_{n}}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}}}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\delta}\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\biggr\},

then it follows from Proposition
S7
that
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\delta}\bigr)\geq 1-2\delta
. Thus, to complete the proof it suffices to show that on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\delta}
we have
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\leq 2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\delta}
. If
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}=1
, then the required bound is immediate. On the other hand, if
\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}>1
, then on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta},\delta}\geq\frac{|\eta(x_{0})-\eta(x_{1})|-\hat{\Delta}_{n}}{\|x_{0}-x_{1}\|_{\infty}^{\hat{\beta}_{n,\delta}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{\beta-\beta_{n}^{\flat}}-\frac{1}{12}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{1-\beta}\geq\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}{2},

since
\epsilon_{1}^{\beta-\beta_{n}^{\flat}}\geq 7/9
by the upper bound on
f(n)
. The result follows.
∎We conclude this section by applying Lemma
S15
to prove Theorem
10
.

We proceed similarly to the proof of Theorem
8
. For
\beta^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,
\lambda^{\prime}\in[1,\infty)
we let
S_{\beta^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}^{\circ}
denote the union
\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}]}B_{(\ell)}
appearing in line 6 of Algorithm
1
when it is applied with
\tilde{\alpha}_{n}
in place of
\alpha
,
\beta^{\prime}
in place of
\beta
and
\lambda^{\prime}
in place of
\lambda
. Furthermore, we let
\hat{A}_{\beta^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}^{\circ}
denote the corresponding output set in
\mathcal{A}
. If
\beta_{0}^{\prime}\leq\beta_{1}^{\prime}
and
\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\geq\lambda_{1}^{\prime}
, then the
p
-values in (
9
) satisfy
\hat{p}_{n,\beta_{1}^{\prime},\lambda_{1}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\leq\hat{p}_{n,\beta_{0}^{\prime},\lambda_{0}^{\prime}}(\cdot)
, and so
S_{\beta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}}^{\circ}\subseteq S_{\beta^{\prime}_{1},\lambda^{\prime}_{1}}^{\circ}
. Consequently,
\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\beta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}}^{\circ})\leq\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\beta^{\prime}_{1},\lambda^{\prime}_{1}}^{\circ})
by line 6 in Algorithm
1
.

(i)
Suppose that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{\dagger}(\beta,\lambda,\lambda_{0},c_{0},r_{0})
. Then by Lemmas
S1
,
S10
and
S15
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime}(\mathcal{D})\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}}^{\circ}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S^{\circ}_{\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}}\nsubseteq S_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}>\beta\bigr)+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}<\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 3\tilde{\alpha}_{n}\leq\alpha,

as required.

(ii)
Now suppose further that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Reg}}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{+}(\beta,{\lambda},\tau,\bm{\epsilon})\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}^{\dagger}(\beta,\lambda,\lambda_{0},c_{0},r_{0})\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
so that by Theorem
9
we have
\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\{\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}<\beta^{\flat}_{n}\}\cup\{\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}>\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\}\bigr)\leq 2\tilde{\alpha}_{n}
, where
\beta^{\flat}_{n}
is defined as in the proof of Lemma
S15
. Moreover, on the event
\{\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}\geq\beta^{\flat}_{n}\}\cap\{\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}\leq\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}\}
, we have

\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime})=\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},2\hat{\lambda}_{n,\hat{\beta}_{n,\tilde{\alpha}_{n}},\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}}^{\circ})\geq\hat{\mu}_{n}(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}).

Hence by Lemma
S36
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\mu(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ})-\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime})\bigr\}\leq 2C_{\mathrm{VC}}~\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}=:\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}.

By Lemma
S1
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(S_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\leq\tilde{\alpha}_{n}
. Observe that we may assume without loss of generality that
(2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta})^{d/\beta}\leq n
, because otherwise the regret bound is vacuous. Thus, combining with the derivation in
(i)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}

\displaystyle\bigl\{\bigl(M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime})\bigr)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}}\bigr\}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big\{M_{\tau}-\mu\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime}\bigr)\big\}+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{\prime\prime}(\mathcal{D})\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big\{M_{\tau}-\mu\bigl(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\bigr)\big\}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}+3\tilde{\alpha}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\bigl(M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ})\bigr)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}}\bigr\}+\mathbb{P}_{P}\bigl(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}+3\tilde{\alpha}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ})\bigm|\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)+4\tilde{\alpha}_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}

\displaystyle=R_{\tau}(\hat{A}_{\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta}}^{\circ})+4\tilde{\alpha}_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\biggl\{\biggl({\frac{(2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta})^{d/\beta_{n}^{\flat}}}{n}\cdot\log_{+}\Bigl(\frac{n}{\tilde{\alpha}_{n}}\Bigr)\biggr)^{\frac{\beta_{n}^{\flat}\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta_{n}^{\flat}+d)+\beta_{n}^{\flat}\gamma}}}+\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\biggr\}+4\tilde{\alpha}_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\biggl\{n^{\frac{2(\beta-\beta^{\flat}_{n})\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta_{n}^{\flat}+d)+\beta_{n}^{\flat}\gamma}}\biggl({\frac{(2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta})^{d/\beta}}{n}\log_{+}\Bigl(\frac{3n^{2}}{\alpha}\Bigr)\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}}\!\!+\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\biggr\}+4\tilde{\alpha}_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\biggl\{f(n)^{\frac{4\gamma(7+2d)}{d}}\biggl({\frac{(2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta})^{d/\beta}}{n}\log_{+}\Bigl(\frac{3n^{2}}{\alpha}\Bigr)\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}}+\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\biggr\}+4\tilde{\alpha}_{n}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{VC}}_{n},

where in the third to last inequality we applied Proposition
5
with
C^{\prime}
in place of
C
by noting that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\tau)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta_{n}^{\flat},2\underline{\lambda}_{\beta},\tau)
. The regret bound on
\hat{A}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{OSS}}
follows by using
(i)
once again.
∎

S5
Proof of the upper bound in Theorem
11

Recall that the upper bound in Theorem
11
will follow from Proposition
13
. First we state the following consequence of Hoeffding’s inequality.

Lemma S16
.

Fix
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,\infty)\times[1,\infty)
and let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=\bigl((X_{i},Y_{i})\bigr)_{i\in[n]}\sim P^{\otimes n}
and let
\mathcal{D}_{X}=(X_{i})_{i\in[n]}
. Given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
h\in[0,1]
, and
\alpha\in(0,1)
define

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}_{x,h}(\alpha):=\begin{cases}\sqrt{e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}}\cdot\biggl(\lambda\cdot h^{\beta}|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\alpha)}{2}}\biggr)&\text{ if }Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\text{ is invertible, }\\
1&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then

\displaystyle\max\bigl\{\mathbb{P}\bigl(\hat{\eta}(x)-\eta(x)\geq\hat{\Delta}_{x,h}(\alpha)\bigm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigr),\mathbb{P}\bigl(\eta(x)-\hat{\eta}(x)\geq\hat{\Delta}_{x,h}(\alpha)\bigm|\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigr)\bigr\}\leq\alpha.

Fix a realisation of
\mathcal{D}_{X}=(X_{i})_{i\in[n]}
. It suffices to restrict our attention to the case where
Q_{x,h}^{\beta}
is invertible. Writing
u_{i}:=\big\langle e_{0},\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})\big\rangle
for
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}\cdot\langle w_{x,h}^{\beta},\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})\rangle

\displaystyle=e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})(w_{x,h}^{\beta})^{\top}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})

\displaystyle=e_{0}^{\top}w_{x,h}^{\beta}

\displaystyle=\eta(x).

Hence,

\displaystyle\langle e_{0},\hat{w}_{x,h}^{\beta}\rangle

\displaystyle=e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}V_{x,h}^{\beta}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}Y_{i}\cdot e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}\cdot\Bigl(\bigl\{Y_{i}-\eta(X_{i})\bigr\}+\bigl\{\eta(X_{i})-\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\beta}[\eta](X_{i})\bigr\}+\big\langle w_{x,h}^{\beta},\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})\big\rangle\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}\cdot\Bigl(\bigl\{Y_{i}-\eta(X_{i})\bigr\}+\bigl\{\eta(X_{i})-\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\beta}[\eta](X_{i})\bigr\}\Bigr)+\eta(x).

(S21)

Note also that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}=e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}.

(S22)

In addition, since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
, for each
i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}
, we have

|\eta(X_{i})-\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\beta}[\eta](X_{i})|\leq\lambda\cdot\|X_{i}-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta}\leq\lambda\cdot h^{\beta},

(S23)

and so by (
S23
), the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
S22
), we have

\displaystyle\biggl|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}\cdot\bigl\{\eta(X_{i})-\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\beta}[\eta](X_{i})\bigr\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\cdot h^{\beta}\cdot\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}|u_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\cdot h^{\beta}\cdot\sqrt{|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|\cdot e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}}.

(S24)

We conclude, by the definition of
\hat{\eta}(x)
, (
S5
), (
S22
), (
S24
) and Hoeffding’s inequality, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\hat{\eta}(x)-\eta(x)\geq\hat{\Delta}_{x,h}(\alpha)\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Biggl(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}\cdot\bigl\{Y_{i}-\eta(X_{i})\bigr\}\geq\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\alpha)}{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{x,h}}u_{i}^{2}}\Biggr)\leq\alpha.

The other inequality follows similarly.
∎

Lemma S17
.

Fix
(\beta,\lambda)\in(0,\infty)\times[1,\infty)
and let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}=\bigl((X_{i},Y_{i})\bigr)_{i\in[n]}\sim P^{\otimes n}
and let
\mathcal{D}_{X}=(X_{i})_{i\in[n]}
. Then for any closed hyper-cube
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B)\leq 1
and
\inf_{x^{\prime}\in B}\eta(x^{\prime})\leq\tau
, and any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\hat{p}_{n}^{+}(B)\leq\alpha\mid\mathcal{D}_{X}\big)\leq\alpha.

Recall that
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
r\in[0,1/2]
denote the centre and
\ell_{\infty}
-radius of
B
, and that
h=(2r)^{1\wedge\frac{1}{\beta}}\in[0,1]
. Again, it restrict our attention to the case where
Q_{x,h}^{\beta}
is invertible. Since
\inf_{x^{\prime}\in B}\eta(x^{\prime})\leq\tau
and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
, we have
\eta(x)\leq\tau+\lambda\cdot r^{\beta\wedge 1}
, and hence the lemma follows from Lemma
S16
.
∎

This follows from Lemma
S17
in the same way as Proposition
4
followed from Lemma
3
.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
S2
, which will rely on several lemmas. For
a>0
, let
\mathcal{K}(a)
denote the set of measurable sets
K\subseteq\bar{B}_{1}(0)
with
\mathcal{L}_{d}(K)\geq a
.

Lemma S18
.

Given
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
\beta\in(0,\infty)
and
a\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle c_{\min}(d,\beta,a):=1\wedge\inf_{K\in\mathcal{K}(a)}\biggl\{\lambda_{\min}\biggl(\int_{K}\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)^{\top}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z)\biggr)\biggr\}>0.

Suppose, for a contradiction, that
c_{\min}(d,\beta,a)=0
. Then we can find a sequence
(K^{(\ell)})_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathcal{K}(a)
, along with a sequence
(w^{(\ell)})_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
with
w^{(\ell)}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{V}(\beta)}
,
\|w^{(\ell)}\|_{2}=1
and

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\int_{K^{(\ell)}}\big\langle w^{(\ell)},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\big\rangle^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\bigl(w^{(\ell)}\bigr)^{\top}\biggl(\int_{K^{(\ell)}}\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)^{\top}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z)\biggr)w^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=0.

(S25)

By moving to a subsequence if necessary, we may assume that
\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}w^{(\ell)}=w^{*}
for some
w^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{V}(\beta)}
with
\|w^{*}\|_{2}=1
. Now since
z\mapsto\langle w^{*},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\rangle
is a non-zero polynomial, the zero-set
\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}:=\bigl\{z\in\bar{B}_{1}(0):\langle w^{*},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\rangle=0\bigr\}
satisfies
\mathcal{L}_{d}(\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}})=0

(e.g.
Okamoto 1973
, Lemma 1)
. In addition, by the continuity of
z\mapsto\langle w^{*},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\rangle
, the set
\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}
is closed. By countable additivity of the finite measure
\mathcal{L}_{d}|_{\bar{B}_{1}(0)}
, there exists
\epsilon_{a}>0
such that
\mathcal{L}_{d}\bigl(\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}^{\epsilon_{a}}\bigr)\leq a/2
where
\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}^{\epsilon_{a}}:=\bigcup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}^{\epsilon_{a}}}B_{\epsilon}(z)=\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}+B_{\epsilon_{a}}(z)
. By continuity again,
\delta_{a}:=\inf_{z\in\bar{B}_{1}(0)\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}^{\epsilon_{a}}}\bigl|\big\langle w^{*},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\big\rangle\bigr|>0
. Now choose
\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large that

\sup_{\ell\geq\ell_{0}}\bigl\|w^{(\ell)}-w^{*}\bigr\|_{2}\leq\frac{\delta_{a}}{2\sqrt{|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}},

so that, by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\bigl|\langle w^{(\ell)},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\rangle\bigr|\geq\bigl|\langle w^{*},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\rangle\bigr|-\bigl|\langle w^{(\ell)}-w^{*},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{a}-\frac{\delta_{a}}{2\sqrt{|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}}\cdot\bigl\|\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta_{a}}{2}

for all
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
and
z\in\bar{B}_{1}(0)\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}^{\epsilon_{a}}
. Hence, for all
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
,

\displaystyle\int_{K^{(\ell)}}\big\langle w^{(\ell)},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\big\rangle^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z)\geq\int_{K^{(\ell)}\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{w^{*}}^{\epsilon_{a}}}\big\langle w^{(\ell)},\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z)\big\rangle^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z)\geq\frac{a\cdot\delta_{a}^{2}}{8}>0,

which contradicts (
S5
), and completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma S19
.

Suppose that
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
\xi\in(0,\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
r\in(0,1/2]
satisfy
\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})\neq\emptyset
. Given any
h\in[2r,1]
, we have
\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\geq\upsilon\cdot\xi\cdot(h/2)^{d}
. In addition, if either
\beta\in(0,1]
or
3r\leq\upsilon h
, then

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\biggl(\int_{\bar{B}_{h}(x)}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)^{\top}\,d\mu(z)\biggr)\geq 2^{-(3d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr),

(S26)

where
c_{\min}^{0}\equiv c_{\min}(d,\beta,2^{-(d+1)}{\upsilon})\in(0,1]
is taken from Lemma
S18
.

First choose
x_{0}\in\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})
, noting that
\bar{B}_{h/2}(x_{0})\subseteq\bar{B}_{h-r}(x_{0})\subseteq\bar{B}_{h}(x)
. Hence, since
x_{0}\in\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\geq\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h-r}(x_{0})\bigr)\geq\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h/2}(x_{0})\bigr)\geq\upsilon\cdot\biggl(\frac{h}{2}\biggr)^{d}\cdot\xi.

For
\beta\in(0,1]
, we have
\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(\cdot)\equiv 1
, so (
S26
) is immediate. Suppose now that
3r\leq\upsilon h
, so that
B_{(1+\upsilon)(h-r)}(x_{0})\supseteq\bar{B}_{h+r}(x_{0})\supseteq\bar{B}_{h}(x)
. Thus, since
x_{0}\in\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)
, we infer that with
M_{x,h}:=\sup_{x^{\prime}\in\bar{B}_{h}(x)}f_{\mu}(x^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\geq\mu\bigl(B_{h-r}(x_{0})\bigr)\geq\upsilon(h-r)^{d}\cdot\sup_{x^{\prime}\in B_{(1+\upsilon)(h-r)}(x_{0})}f_{\mu}(x^{\prime})\geq\upsilon\cdot\biggl(\frac{h}{2}\biggr)^{d}\cdot M_{x,h}.

(S27)

Moreover, if we take
J_{x,h}:=\bigl\{x^{\prime}\in\bar{B}_{h}(x):f_{\mu}(x^{\prime})\geq 2^{-(2d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot M_{x,h}\bigr\}
, then

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}_{d}(J_{x,h})\cdot M_{x,h}+\mathcal{L}_{d}\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\setminus J_{x,h}\bigr)\cdot 2^{-(2d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot M_{x,h}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}_{d}(J_{x,h})\cdot M_{x,h}+\frac{\upsilon}{2}\cdot\biggl(\frac{h}{2}\biggr)^{d}\cdot M_{x,h},

so by (
S27
) we have
\mathcal{L}_{d}(J_{x,h})\geq 2^{-(d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot h^{d}
. Taking
K_{x,h}:=h^{-1}\cdot(J_{x,h}-x)\subseteq\bar{B}_{1}(0)
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{d}(K_{x,h})\geq 2^{-(d+1)}\cdot\upsilon
. Given any
w\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{V}(\beta)}
with
\|w\|_{2}=1
, it follows from Lemma
S18
that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{B}_{h}(x)}\big\langle w,\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)\big\rangle^{2}\,d\mu(z)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-(2d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot M_{x,h}\cdot\int_{J_{x,h}}\big\langle w,\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)\big\rangle^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-(2d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot M_{x,h}\cdot h^{d}\cdot\int_{K_{x,h}}\big\langle w,\Phi_{0,1}^{\beta}(z^{\prime})\big\rangle^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}_{d}(z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-(3d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr).

The result follows.
∎

Lemma S20
.

Suppose that
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
\xi\in(0,\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
r\in(0,1/2]
and
h\in[2r,1]
satisfy
\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})\neq\emptyset
, and choose
\delta\in(0,1)
. Suppose also that

\displaystyle h\geq\biggl\{\frac{2^{4(d+1)}\cdot|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\upsilon^{2}\cdot\xi\cdot n}\cdot\log\biggl(\frac{2|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{\delta}\biggr)\biggr\}^{1/d},

(S28)

and that either
\beta\in(0,1]
or
3r\leq\upsilon h
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\bigl\{|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|\leq 2n\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\bigr\}\cap\bigl\{\lambda_{\min}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)\geq 2^{-(3d+2)}\cdot n\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\upsilon\cdot

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\delta.

By Lemma
S19
and (
S28
), we have
\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\geq\upsilon\cdot\xi\cdot(h/2)^{d}\geq(8/3)\log(2/\delta)/n
. Hence, by the multiplicative Chernoff bound (Lemma
S39
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl\{|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|>2n\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\bigr\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

(S29)

In addition, if either
\beta\in(0,1]
or
3r\leq\upsilon h
, then by Lemma
S19
again,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\biggl(\int_{\bar{B}_{h}(x)}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)^{\top}\,d\mu(z)\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-(3d+1)}\cdot\upsilon\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-(4d+1)}\cdot\upsilon^{2}\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\xi\cdot h^{d}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{8|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{n}\cdot\log\biggl(\frac{2|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{\delta}\biggr).

Note also that
\lambda_{\max}\bigl(\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{1})\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{1})^{\top}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{1}\in\bar{B}_{h}(x)\}}\bigr)\leq|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|
. Hence, by a matrix multiplicative Chernoff bound (Lemma
S40
) applied with
m=n
,
\mathbf{Z}_{i}=\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(X_{i})^{\top}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{i}\in\bar{B}_{h}(x)\}}
and
q=|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\lambda_{\min}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)

\displaystyle<2^{-(3d+2)}\cdot n\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\upsilon\cdot\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\lambda_{\min}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)<\frac{n}{2}\cdot\lambda_{\min}\biggl(\int_{\bar{B}_{h}(x)}\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)\Phi_{x,h}^{\beta}(z)^{\top}d\mu(z)\biggr)\biggr\}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

(S30)

The result now follows by combining (
S29
) and (
S5
) with a union bound.
∎

Lemma S21
.

Suppose that
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\beta>0
,
\lambda\geq 1
,
\kappa,\gamma>0
,
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
C_{\mathrm{App}}\geq 1
, take
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)\cap\mathcal{P}^{+}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
and let
{C}_{\mathrm{pv}}:=2^{2d+5}\cdot\bigl(\upsilon\cdot\sqrt{c_{\min}^{0}}\bigr)^{-1}
. Suppose further that
\xi,\Delta\in(0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
r\in(0,1/2]
satisfy
\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\neq\emptyset
, where
\mu
is the marginal distribution of
P
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
is the regression function. Given any
\delta\in(0,1)
with

\displaystyle r\geq\biggl\{\frac{{C}_{\mathrm{pv}}^{2}\cdot|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{\xi\cdot n}\cdot\log\biggl(\frac{4|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{\delta}\biggr)\biggr\}^{\frac{\beta}{d(\beta\wedge 1)}},\ \ \Delta\geq C_{\mathrm{pv}}\Biggl(\lambda r^{\beta\wedge 1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log\bigl(2/(\alpha\wedge\delta)\bigr)}{\xi\cdot n\cdot r^{d(\beta\wedge 1)/\beta}}}\Biggr),

and either
\beta\in(0,1]
or
r\leq\{2(\upsilon/3)^{\beta}\}^{\frac{1}{{\beta-1}}}
, we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\hat{p}_{n}^{+}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r}(x)\bigr)\leq\alpha\bigr\}\geq 1-\delta
.

First recall that in the construction of our
p
-values
\hat{p}_{n}^{+}(\cdot)
in (
19
) we take
h=(2r)^{1\wedge\frac{1}{\beta}}
. To prove the lemma we define events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\eta}

\displaystyle:=\biggl\{\hat{\eta}(x)>\eta(x)-\sqrt{e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{+}e_{0}}\cdot\biggl(\lambda\cdot h^{\beta}\cdot|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2}}\biggr)\biggr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\mu}

\displaystyle:=\biggl\{\lambda_{\min}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)\geq 2^{-(3d+2)}\cdot c_{\min}^{0}\cdot\upsilon^{2}\cdot\max\biggl\{\xi\cdot n\cdot\biggl(\frac{h}{2}\biggr)^{d},\frac{|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|}{2\upsilon}\biggr\}\biggr\}.

Note that since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
and
\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\neq\emptyset
, we have
\eta(x)\geq\tau+\Delta-\lambda\cdot r^{\beta\wedge 1}
. Hence, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\eta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\mu}
we have

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}

\displaystyle(x)-\tau-\lambda\Bigl(1+2\sqrt{e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}\cdot|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|}\Bigr)r^{\beta\wedge 1}

\displaystyle>\eta(x)-\tau-\lambda\Bigl(1+4\sqrt{e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}\cdot|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|}\Bigr)r^{\beta\wedge 1}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\cdot e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}\cdot\log(2/\delta)}

\displaystyle>\Delta-2\lambda\Bigl(1+2\sqrt{e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}\cdot|\mathcal{N}_{x,h}|}\Bigr)r^{\beta\wedge 1}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\cdot e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}\cdot\log(2/\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta-2\lambda\biggl(1+\sqrt{\frac{2^{3d+5}}{c^{0}_{\min}\cdot\upsilon}}\biggr)r^{\beta\wedge 1}-\sqrt{\frac{2^{4d+1}\cdot\log(2/\delta)}{c^{0}_{\min}\cdot\upsilon^{2}\cdot\xi\cdot n\cdot r^{d(\beta\wedge 1)/\beta}}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{2^{4d+1}\cdot\log(2/\alpha)}{c^{0}_{\min}\cdot\upsilon^{2}\cdot\xi\cdot n\cdot r^{d(\beta\wedge 1)/\beta}}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\cdot e_{0}^{\top}\bigl(Q_{x,h}^{\beta}\bigr)^{-1}e_{0}\cdot\log(1/\alpha)}.

Hence, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\eta}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\mu}
we have
\hat{p}_{n}^{+}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r}(x)\bigr)\leq\alpha
. Now by Lemma
S16
we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\eta})^{\mathrm{c}}\cap E_{\delta}^{\mu}\bigr)\leq\delta/2
. Moreover, by Lemma
S19
we have
\mu\bigl(\bar{B}_{h}(x)\bigr)\geq\upsilon\cdot\xi\cdot(h/2)^{d}
. Hence, by Lemma
S20
we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\mu})^{\mathrm{c}}\bigr)\leq\delta/2
. Thus
\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\eta})^{\mathrm{c}}\cup(E_{\delta}^{\mu})^{\mathrm{c}}\bigr)\leq\delta
, and the conclusion follows.
∎Given any
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
\xi
,
\Delta\in(0,\infty)
,
r\in(0,1/2]
, we let

{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r):=\bigl\{B\in\mathcal{H}^{+}:\mathrm{diam}_{\infty}(B)=2r\text{ and }B\cap\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\neq\emptyset\bigr\}.

Lemma S22
.

We have
|{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r)|\leq(2/r)^{d}\cdot(\upsilon\cdot\xi)^{-1}
for every
\upsilon\in(0,1)
,
\xi
,
\Delta\in(0,\infty)
and
r\in(0,1/2]
.

Given
B=2r\prod_{j\in[d]}[a_{j},a_{j}+1]\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r)
, for some
(a_{j})_{j\in[d]}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we write
\phi(B):=(a_{j}\mod 2)_{j\in[d]}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
and
\psi(B):=r\prod_{j\in[d]}[2a_{j}-1,2a_{j}+3]
. Note that if
\phi(B_{0})=\phi(B_{1})
for distinct
B_{0}
,
B_{1}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r)
then
\mu\bigl(\psi(B_{0})\cap\psi(B_{1})\bigr)=0
, since
\mu
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Moreover, by Lemma
S19
we have
\mu\bigl(\psi(B)\bigr)\geq\upsilon\cdot\xi\cdot r^{d}
, so

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r)|\cdot\upsilon\xi r^{d}\leq\sum_{B\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r)}\mu\bigl(\psi(B)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{d}}~\sum_{B\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r)\cap\phi^{-1}\{z\}}\mu\bigl(\psi(B)\bigr)\leq 2^{d},

as required.
∎

Let

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle:=\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma,\quad\theta:=\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}}{n}\log_{+}\biggl(\frac{8n^{\frac{\beta}{\beta\wedge 1}}|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|\log_{2}n}{\alpha\wedge\delta}\biggr),

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle:=\theta^{\beta\gamma/\rho},\quad r_{*}:=2^{-\big\lfloor\frac{1}{\beta\wedge 1}\{\frac{\beta\kappa}{\rho}\log_{2}(1/\theta)+\log_{2}\lambda\}\big\rfloor},

and, recalling the definition of
{C}_{\mathrm{pv}}:=2^{2d+5}\cdot\bigl(\upsilon\cdot\sqrt{c_{\min}^{0}}\bigr)^{-1}
from Lemma
S21
, define

\displaystyle A_{0}:=\begin{cases}2\vee C_{\mathrm{pv}}|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|^{1/2}\vee 2^{\frac{\beta-2}{\beta-1}}(3/\upsilon)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}&\text{ if }\beta>1\\
2^{\beta}\vee C_{\mathrm{pv}}|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|^{1/2}&\text{ if }\beta\leq 1.\end{cases}

Then for
\theta^{\beta}\leq A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\kappa}}
we have

\biggl(\frac{C_{\mathrm{pv}}^{2}\cdot|\mathcal{V}(\beta)|}{\xi}\cdot\theta\lambda^{-d/\beta}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta}{d(\beta\wedge 1)}}\leq r_{*}\leq\frac{1}{2},

and
r_{*}\leq\{2(\upsilon/3)^{\beta}\}^{\frac{1}{{\beta-1}}}
if
\beta>1
. Now let

\Delta:=C_{\mathrm{pv}}\Biggl(\lambda r_{*}^{\beta\wedge 1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log\bigl(2/(\alpha\wedge\delta)\bigr)}{\xi\cdot n\cdot r_{*}^{d(\beta\wedge 1)/\beta}}}\Biggr),

so that
\Delta\leq 3\cdot C_{\mathrm{pv}}\theta^{\frac{\beta\kappa}{\rho}}
when
\theta\leq A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\beta\kappa}}
. By Lemma
S22
, we have
|{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r_{*})|\leq(2/r_{*})^{d}\cdot(\upsilon\cdot\xi)^{-1}\leq(\lambda^{d}\theta^{-\beta})^{\frac{1}{\beta\wedge 1}}\leq n^{\frac{\beta}{\beta\wedge 1}}
when
\theta\leq A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\beta\kappa}}
. Hence we may apply a union bound and Lemma
S21
with
\delta/(2n^{\frac{\beta}{\beta\wedge 1}})
in place of
\delta
and
\alpha/(n\log_{2}n)
in place of
\alpha
to deduce that whenever
\theta\leq A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\beta\kappa}}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\bigcup_{B\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r_{*})}\biggl\{\hat{p}_{n}^{+}(B)>\frac{\alpha}{|\mathcal{H}^{+}(\mathcal{D}_{X})|}\biggr\}\biggr)\leq\sum_{B\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r_{*})}\mathbb{P}\biggl(\hat{p}_{n}^{+}(B)>\frac{\alpha}{n\log_{2}n}\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

Hence, whenever
\theta\leq A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\beta\kappa}}
, we have

\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\subseteq\bigcup_{B\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\upsilon}(\xi,\Delta,r_{*})}B\subseteq\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)},

with probability at least
1-\delta/2
. Thus, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle M_{\tau}-\sup\biggl\{\mu(A):A\in

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\biggl(\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq M_{\tau}-\sup\Bigl\{\mu(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({f_{\mu}})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta\leq A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\beta\kappa}}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta>A_{0}^{-\frac{\rho}{\beta\kappa}}\}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\bigl\{1+(3C_{\mathrm{pv}})^{\gamma}+A_{0}^{\gamma}\bigr\}\cdot\theta^{\beta\kappa\gamma/\rho}.

(S31)

Finally, since
\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{+}
is chosen from
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\bigcup_{\ell\in[\ell_{\alpha}]}B_{(\ell)}\bigr)
with maximal empirical measure, it follows from Lemma
S36
as in the proof of Proposition
S2
that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle M_{\tau}-\mu(\hat{A}_{\mathrm{OSS}}^{+})\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}

\displaystyle\bigl\{1+(3C_{\mathrm{pv}})^{\gamma}+A_{0}^{\gamma}\bigr\}\cdot\theta^{\beta\kappa\gamma/\rho}

\displaystyle+2C_{\mathrm{VC}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{dim}_{\mathrm{VC}}(\mathcal{A})}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2/\delta)}{n}}.

The second part of Proposition
13
follows integrating the tail bound and applying Proposition
12
, as at the end of the proof of Theorem
5
.
∎

S6
Proofs of the lower bounds in Theorems
2
and
11

Recall the construction of the probability distributions
\{P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}:\ell\in[L]\}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}
from Section
5
, with corresponding regression functions
\{\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}:\ell\in[L]\}
and common marginal distribution
\mu_{L,r,w}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Recall also the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\cdot)
from (
15
). Our initial goal is to prove that
\{P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}:\ell\in[L]\}
is a subset of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
(see Lemma
S28
) and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})\cap\mathcal{P}^{+}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
(see Lemma
S30
) for suitable
L
,
r
,
w
,
s
and
\theta
. The first of these lemmas will rely on several auxiliary results,

Given two multi-indices
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top},\nu^{\prime}=(\nu_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\nu_{d}^{\prime})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, we write
\nu\prec\nu^{\prime}
if either
\|\nu\|_{1}<\|\nu^{\prime}\|_{1}
or both
\|\nu\|_{1}=\|\nu^{\prime}\|_{1}
and there exists
j\in\{0,1,\ldots,d-1\}
such that
\nu_{1}=\nu^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\nu_{j}=\nu^{\prime}_{j}
and
\nu_{j+1}<\nu^{\prime}_{j+1}
. Now, given
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in[m]
, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{j}

\displaystyle(\nu,m)

\displaystyle:=\biggl\{\!(k_{1},\ldots,k_{j},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{j})\in\mathbb{N}^{j}\times(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d})^{j}\!:0\!\prec\!\ell_{1}\!\prec\!\ldots\!\prec\!\ell_{j},\sum_{q=1}^{j}k_{q}=m,\sum_{q=1}^{j}k_{q}\ell_{q}=\nu\biggr\}.

In addition, for multi-indices
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, we let
\nu!:=\prod_{m=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\nu_{j}!
. The following lemma is a version of the Faà di Bruno formula.

Lemma S23
(Corollary 2.10 of
Constantine and
Savits 1996
).

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
. Suppose that all partial derivative of order
\|\nu\|_{1}
of
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
exist and are continuous in a neighbourhood of
x
, and that
g:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is
\|\nu\|_{1}
-times continuously differentiable in a neighbourhood of
f(x)
. Then

\displaystyle\partial^{\nu}_{x}(g\circ f)=\nu!\cdot\sum_{m=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}g^{(m)}\bigl(f(x)\bigr)\sum_{j=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{j},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{j})\\
\in\mathcal{Q}_{j}(\nu,m)\end{subarray}}\prod_{q=1}^{j}\frac{\{\partial^{\ell_{q}}_{x}(f)\}^{k_{q}}}{k_{q}!\cdot(\ell_{q}!)^{k_{q}}}.

Lemma S24
.

Given
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, we have
\sup_{\|x\|_{2}\geq 1}|\partial^{\nu}_{x}(\|\cdot\|_{2})|<\infty
.

For
t\in[d]
, write
e_{t}=(0,\ldots,0,1,0,\ldots,0)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
for the
t^{\mathrm{th}}
standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By Lemma
S23
with
f(x)=\|x\|_{2}^{2}
and
g(z)=\sqrt{z}
, we have for
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that

\displaystyle\partial^{\nu}_{x}(\|\cdot\|_{2})

\displaystyle=\nu!\sum_{m=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\frac{(-1)^{m+1}(2m\!-\!3)!!}{2^{m}\|x\|_{2}^{2m-1}}\sum_{j=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{j},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{j})\\
\in\mathcal{Q}_{j}(\nu,m)\end{subarray}}\!\prod_{q=1}^{j}\frac{2^{k_{q}}\bigl\{\sum_{t=1}^{d}(x_{t}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\ell_{q}=e_{t}\}}\!+\!\mathbbm{1}_{\{\ell_{q}=2e_{t}\}})\bigr\}^{k_{q}}}{k_{q}!\cdot(\ell_{q}!)^{k_{q}}}.

It follows that for all
{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
with
\|x\|_{2}\geq 1
we have

\displaystyle|\partial^{\nu}_{x}(\|\cdot\|_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq\nu!\sum_{m=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\frac{(2m\!-\!3)!!}{\|x\|_{2}^{2m-1}}\sum_{j=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{j},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{j})\\
\in\mathcal{Q}_{j}(\nu,m)\end{subarray}}\prod_{q=1}^{j}\frac{\|x\|_{2}^{k_{q}}}{k_{q}!\cdot(\ell_{q}!)^{k_{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq\nu!\sum_{m=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}(2m\!-\!3)!!\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\|\nu\|_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{j},\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{j})\\
\in\mathcal{Q}_{j}(\nu,m)\end{subarray}}\prod_{q=1}^{j}\frac{1}{k_{q}!\cdot(\ell_{q}!)^{k_{q}}}<\infty,

as required.
∎

Lemma S25
.

For each
m,d\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{m,d}>0
, depending only on
m
and
d
, such that for any infinitely differentiable function
g:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
with
g^{\prime}(z)=0
for all
z\in[0,1]
, and any
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\nu\|_{1}=m
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(g\circ\|\cdot\|_{2})\bigr|\leq C_{m,d}\cdot\max_{k\in[m]}\sup_{z\in[0,\infty)}|g^{(k)}(z)|

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The lemma follows from combining Lemmas
S23
and
S24
, and by considering the cases
\|x\|_{2}<1
and
\|x\|_{2}\geq 1
separately.
∎

Lemma S26
.

Let
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\in(0,\infty)
,
s\in(0,1\wedge(r/2)]
,
w\in\bigl(0,(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr)
and
\theta\in(0,\epsilon_{0}/2]
. Then for
\ell\in[L]
,
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\nu\|_{1}=m
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}\cdot\frac{\theta}{s^{m}},

(S32)

where
A_{m}
is taken from (
21
) and
C_{m,d}
is taken from Lemma
S25
.
Hence, given any
\xi\in[0,1]
and
x
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})-\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{m+1}(2C_{m,d}\vee dC_{m+1,d})\cdot\frac{\theta}{s^{m+\xi}}\cdot\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\xi}.

(S33)

To prove (
S32
), we construct an open cover of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
\{U_{1},\ldots,U_{L+1}\}
where
U_{\ell^{\prime}}:=B_{r_{\sharp}(w)}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})
for
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
and
U_{L+1}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[L]}\bar{B}_{2d^{1/2}r}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})
. First suppose that
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]\backslash\{\ell\}
and consider the function
g_{0}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
defined by

\displaystyle g_{0}(t):=\begin{cases}\tau-\theta&\text{if }t\leq 1\\
\tau+\theta-2\theta\cdot h\bigl(t\!-\!1\bigr)&\text{if }1<t\leq 2\\
\tau+\theta&\text{if }2<t\leq\frac{d^{1/2}r}{s}\\
\tau-\theta+2\theta\cdot h\bigl(\frac{s\cdot t}{d^{1/2}r}\!-\!1\bigr)&\text{if }\frac{d^{1/2}r}{s}<t<\frac{2d^{1/2}r}{s}\\
\tau-\theta&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

By Lemma
S25
, together with
s\leq r/2\leq d^{1/2}r
, we have

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(g_{0}\circ\|\cdot\|_{2})\bigr|\leq C_{m,d}\cdot\max_{k\in[m]}\sup_{z\in[0,\infty)}|g_{0}^{(k)}(z)|\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}\theta.

Moreover, for all
x\in U_{\ell^{\prime}}
we have
\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}(x)=g_{0}(\|s^{-1}\cdot(x-{z}_{\ell^{\prime}})\|_{2})
. Hence, for all
{x\in U_{\ell^{\prime}}}
we have
\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})\bigr|\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}{\theta}s^{-m}
since
\|\nu\|_{1}=m
. Next, consider the open set
U_{\ell}
and define a function
g_{1}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
by

\displaystyle g_{1}(t)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\tau+\theta&\text{if }t\leq 1\\
\tau-\theta+2\theta\cdot h\bigl(t\!-\!1\bigr)&\text{if }1<t<2\\
\tau-\theta&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

By applying Lemma
S25
again, we have
\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(g_{1}\circ\|\cdot\|_{2})\bigr|\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}{\theta}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Moreover, for
x\in U_{\ell}
we have
\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}(x)=g_{1}(\|(d^{1/2}r)^{-1}\cdot(x-{z}_{\ell})\|_{2})
. Hence, for all
{x\in U_{\ell}}
, we have
\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})\bigr|\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}{\theta}(d^{1/2}r)^{-m}\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}{\theta}s^{-m}
. Finally we note that
\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}\big|_{U_{L+1}}\equiv\tau-\theta
, so
\sup_{x\in U_{L+1}}\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell})\bigr|=0\leq 2A_{m}C_{m,d}{\theta}s^{-m}
. The claim (
S32
) follows.

To prove (
S33
), we first consider the case where
\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq s
, in which case, we may apply the mean value theorem combined with (
S32
) and Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta})-\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{\nu}(\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq dA_{m+1}C_{m+1,d}\cdot\frac{\theta}{s^{m+1}}\cdot\|{x-x^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq dA_{m+1}C_{m+1,d}\cdot\frac{\theta}{s^{m+\xi}}\cdot\|{x-x^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}^{\xi}.

Moreover, when
\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}>s
, (
S33
) follows immediately from (
S32
) and the triangle inequality.
∎

Lemma S27
.

Take
\beta>0
,
C_{f}>0
and let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a
\lceil\beta\rceil
-times differentiable function such that for all
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\|\nu\|_{1}=\lceil\beta\rceil-1=:m
, and
x
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{\nu}(f)-\partial_{x}^{\nu}(f)\bigr|\leq C_{f}\cdot\|x^{\prime}-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta-m}.

Then for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\bigl|f(x^{\prime})-\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\beta}[f](x^{\prime})\bigr|\leq C_{f}\cdot\binom{m+d-1}{d-1}\cdot\|x^{\prime}-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta}.

By Taylor’s theorem, there exists
t\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle f(x^{\prime})=\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\nu\|_{1}<m}\frac{(x^{\prime}-x)^{\nu}}{\nu!}\cdot\partial^{\nu}_{x}(f)+\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\nu\|_{1}=m}\frac{(x^{\prime}-x)^{\nu}}{\nu!}\cdot\partial^{\nu}_{x+t\cdot(x^{\prime}-x)}(f).

Hence,

\displaystyle\bigl|f(x^{\prime})-\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\beta}[f](x^{\prime})\bigr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|\nu\|_{1}=m}\frac{(x^{\prime}-x)^{\nu}}{\nu!}\cdot\bigl(\partial^{\nu}_{x+t\cdot(x^{\prime}-x)}(f)-\partial^{\nu}_{x}(f)\bigr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq C_{f}\cdot\binom{m+d-1}{d-1}\cdot\|x^{\prime}-x\|_{\infty}^{\beta},

as required.
∎

Lemma S28
.

Let
\beta>0
,
\lambda>1
,
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\in(0,\infty)
,
s\in(0,1\wedge(r/2)]
,
w\in\bigl(0,(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr)
and
\theta\in(0,\epsilon_{0}/2]
. There exists
c^{\flat}_{\beta,d}>0
, depending only on
\beta
and
d
, such that whenever
\theta\leq c^{\flat}_{\beta,d}\cdot\lambda\cdot s^{\beta}
, we have that for each
\ell\in[L]
, the function
\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
is
(\beta,\lambda)
-Hölder on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
; i.e.
P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
.

By taking

c^{\flat}_{\beta,d}:=\min_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:q\leq\lceil\beta\rceil-1}\biggl\{2A_{q+1}(2C_{q,d}\vee dC_{q+1,d})\cdot\binom{q+d-1}{d-1}\biggr\}^{-1},

the result follows from Lemmas
S26
and
S27
.
∎Lemma
S30
also requires one auxiliary lemma.

Lemma S29
.

Given
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>0
and
w\in\bigl(0,(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr)
, we have
\omega_{\mu_{L,r,w},d}(x)\geq\frac{w}{L\cdot(4d^{1/2})^{d}}
for all
x\in\bigcup_{\ell\in[L]}K^{0}_{r}(\ell)
. Moreover,
\bigcup_{\ell\in[L]}K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu_{L,r,w})
for every
\upsilon\leq(4d^{1/2})^{-d}
.

Let
\ell\in[L]
, let
x\in K^{0}_{r}(\ell)=\bar{B}_{r}(z_{\ell})
and let
\tilde{r}\in(0,1)
. If
\tilde{r}\in(0,2r]
, then
\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell)
contains a hyper-cube of radius
\tilde{r}/2
, so
\mu_{L,r,w}\bigl(\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\bigr)\geq w\cdot L^{-1}\cdot\tilde{r}^{d}
. Consequently, when
\tilde{r}\in\bigl(0,8d^{1/2}r\bigr]
, we have

\mu_{L,r,w}\bigl(\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\bigr)\geq\mu_{L,r,w}\bigl(\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}/(4d^{1/2})}(x)\bigr)\geq\frac{w}{L\cdot(4d^{1/2})^{d}}\cdot\tilde{r}^{d}.

Note also that since
x\in K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\subseteq\bar{B}_{2d^{1/2}r}(z_{\ell})
there exists
\sigma_{x}\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
and
\tilde{x}=z_{\ell}+\sigma_{x}\cdot 2d^{1/2}r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\tilde{x}-x\|_{\infty}\leq 2d^{1/2}r
. Hence, if
\tilde{r}\in(4d^{1/2}r,r_{\sharp}(w)]
, then
\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}/4}({z}_{\ell}+\sigma_{x}\cdot(2d^{1/2}r+\tilde{r}/4))\subseteq\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\cap K^{1}_{r}(\ell)
. Thus, we have
\mu_{L,r,w}\bigl(\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\bigr)\geq(w/L)\cdot(\tilde{r}/2)^{d}
for
\tilde{r}\in(4d^{1/2}r,r_{\sharp}(w)]
, and consequently,
\mu_{L,r,w}\bigl(\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\bigr)\geq(w/L)\cdot(\tilde{r}/4)^{d}
for
\tilde{r}\in(8d^{1/2}r,2r_{\sharp}(w)]
. Finally, if
\tilde{r}\in(2r_{\sharp}(w),1)
, then
K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\cup K_{r}^{1}(\ell)\subseteq\bar{B}_{r_{\sharp}(w)}({z}_{\ell})\subseteq\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)
so
\mu_{L,r,w}\bigl(\bar{B}_{\tilde{r}}(x)\bigr)\geq 1/L>(w/L)\cdot\tilde{r}^{d}
. The first conclusion of the lemma therefore follows. The second part then follows from the fact that the Lebesgue density of
\mu_{L,r,w}
is at most
w/L
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
∎

Lemma S30
.

Let
\beta>0
,
\lambda>1
,
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\in(0,\infty)
,
s\in(0,1\wedge(r/2)]
,
w\in\bigl(0,(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr)
and
\theta\in(0,\epsilon_{0}/2]
,
\ell\in[L]
,
\upsilon\leq(4d^{1/2})^{-d}
and let
\eta=\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
,
\mu=\mu_{L,r,w}
and
P=P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
. Suppose also that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{conv}}
. Given any
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
and
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
with
A\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
and
{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\notin A
for some
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
, we have
\mu(A)\leq(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}/2
. In particular,
\mu(A)\leq(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}/2
whenever
A\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
for some
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
and
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]\setminus\{\ell\}
. Moreover,
M_{\tau}(P,\mathcal{A})=\mu\bigl(K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\bigr)=(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}
. Finally, if
(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\min\big\{(w/\{(4d^{1/2})^{d}\cdot L\})^{\kappa},\theta^{\gamma}\big\}
, then
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})\cap\mathcal{P}^{+}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
.

First take
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
and
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
with
A\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
and
{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\notin A
. Since
\eta(x)=\tau-\theta
for all
x\in K^{1}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})
, we must have
A\cap\bigl(K^{1}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\cup\{{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\}\bigr)=\emptyset
. Moreover,
A
is convex with
A\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
, and it follows that
A\subseteq\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}~:~\|x-{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}<2d^{1/2}r\}
. Thus
A\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)=A\cap\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}~:~\|x-{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}<2d^{1/2}r\}\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)=A\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})
is the intersection of two axis-aligned hyper-rectangles, so is itself an axis-aligned hyper-rectangle. Since
A\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\subseteq K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\setminus\{{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\}=\bar{B}_{r}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})\setminus\{{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\}
, we deduce that
\mu(A)\leq(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}/2
. In particular, if
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]\setminus\{\ell\}
, then
\eta({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})=\tau-\theta
, so
{z}_{\ell^{\prime}}\notin\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
and the conclusion
\mu(A)\leq(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}/2
holds.

For the next part, note that
K^{0}_{r}(\ell)=\bar{B}_{r}({z}_{\ell})\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)\subseteq\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
since
\eta(x)=\tau+\theta
for all
x\in K^{0}_{r}(\ell)
. Hence,
M_{\tau}(P,\mathcal{A})\geq\mu\big(K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\big)=(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}
. On the other hand, given
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr)
, we have either
A\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\subseteq K^{0}_{r}(\ell)
, in which case
\mu(A)\leq\mu\big(K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\big)=(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}
, or
A\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap K^{0}_{r}(\ell^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
for some
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]\setminus\{\ell\}
, since
\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\subseteq\bigcup_{\ell\in[L]}K^{0}_{r}(\ell)
, in which case
\mu(A)\leq(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}/2
. Hence
M_{\tau}(P,\mathcal{A})=(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}
.

For the final part, assume that
(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\min\big\{\{w/(4^{d}d^{1/2}\cdot L)\}^{\kappa},\theta^{\gamma}\big\}
, and fix
(\xi,\Delta)\in(0,\infty)^{2}
. We consider two cases: first suppose that
\xi\leq{w}/\{L\cdot(4d^{1/2})^{d}\}
and
\Delta\leq\theta
. By Lemma
S29
, we have
K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\xi}({\omega_{\mu,d}})
. Moreover, it follows from the construction of
\eta
that
K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\theta}(\eta)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)
. Thus, with
A_{\xi,\Delta}=K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\mu,d})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)
, we have

\displaystyle\mu(A_{\xi,\Delta})=\frac{w}{L}\cdot(2r)^{d}\geq\frac{w}{L}\cdot(2r)^{d}-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma})=M_{\tau}-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma}).

On the other hand, if
\xi>{w}/\{L\cdot(4d^{1/2})^{d}\}
or
\Delta>\theta
, then with
A_{\xi,\Delta}=\emptyset\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\mu,d})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)
, we have

\displaystyle\mu(A_{\xi,\Delta})=0\geq\frac{w}{L}\cdot(2r)^{d}-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{w}{(4d^{1/2})^{d}\cdot L}\biggr)^{\kappa}\!\!,\theta^{\gamma}\biggr\}\geq M_{\tau}-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma}).

We conclude that
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
. To prove
P\in\mathcal{P}^{+}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
, we proceed similarly using the facts that
K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu)
by Lemma
S29
and that
\mu
has Lebesgue density at least
w/L
on
K^{0}_{r}(\ell)
.
∎Lemma
S31
below bounds the
\chi^{2}
-divergence between pairs of distributions in our class
\{P_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}:\ell\in[L]\}
.

Lemma S31
.

Suppose that
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1/2)
,
\tau\in[\epsilon_{0},1-\epsilon_{0}]
,
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\in(0,\infty)
,
s\in(0,1\wedge(r/2)]
,
w\in\bigl(0,(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr)
and
\theta\in(0,\epsilon_{0}/2]
. Then

\chi^{2}\big(P^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta},P^{\ell^{\prime}}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}\big)\leq\frac{2^{5+2d}\cdot w\cdot\theta^{2}\cdot s^{d}}{\epsilon_{0}\cdot L}

for all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
.

Let
Q_{L,r,w}:=\mu_{L,r,w}\times m_{\mathrm{ct}}
where
m_{\mathrm{ct}}
denotes the counting measure on
\{0,1\}
. Note that
P^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}
is absolutely continuous with respect to
Q_{L,r,w}
, for all
\ell\in[L]
. Given
\ell\in[L]
, define
p_{\ell}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle p_{\ell}(x,y):

\displaystyle=\frac{dP^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}}{dQ_{L,r,w}}(x,y)=(1-y)\cdot\bigl(1-\eta_{L,r,\theta}^{\ell}(x)\bigr)+y\cdot\eta_{L,r,\theta}^{\ell}(x).

Take
\ell,~\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
and observe that
\eta^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}(x)=\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}}(x)
for all
x\in J_{L,r,w}\setminus\big(\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell})\cup\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})\big)
. Note also that
\mu_{L,r,w}\big(\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell})\cup\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})\big)=(2w/L)\cdot(4s)^{d}
; moreover,
\eta_{L,r,\theta}^{\ell}(x)\in[\tau-\theta,\tau+\theta]\subseteq[\epsilon_{0}/2,1-\epsilon_{0}/2]
for all
x\in J_{L,r,w}
and
\ell\in[L]
. Hence,

\displaystyle\chi^{2}

\displaystyle(P^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta},P^{\ell^{\prime}}_{L,r,w,s,\theta})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}}\biggl(\frac{dP^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}}{dP^{\ell^{\prime}}_{L,r,\theta}}-1\biggr)^{2}dP^{\ell^{\prime}}_{L,r,\theta}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}}\biggl(\frac{p_{\ell}(x,y)}{p_{\ell^{\prime}}(x,y)}-1\biggr)^{2}p_{\ell^{\prime}}(x,y)\,dQ_{L,r,w}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}}\frac{\{p_{\ell}(x,y)-p_{\ell^{\prime}}(x,y)\}^{2}}{p_{\ell^{\prime}}(x,y)}\,dQ_{L,r,w}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\biggl(\frac{\{\eta^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}(x)-\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\}^{2}}{1-\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}}(x)}+\frac{\{\eta^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}(x)-\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\}^{2}}{\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}}(x)}\biggr)\,d\mu_{L,r,w}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\epsilon_{0}}\int_{\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell})\cup\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})}\bigl\{\eta^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}(x)-\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\bigr\}^{2}\,d\mu_{L,r,w}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\epsilon_{0}}\cdot(2\theta)^{2}\cdot\mu_{L,r,w}\big(\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell})\cup\bar{B}_{2s}({z}_{\ell^{\prime}})\big)=\frac{2^{5+2d}\cdot w\cdot\theta^{2}\cdot s^{d}}{\epsilon_{0}\cdot L},

as required.
∎We are now in a position to state the crucial proposition for the proof of Proposition
14
.

Proposition S32
.

Assume that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hpr}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{conv}}
. Fix
(\beta,\gamma,\kappa,\lambda,C_{\mathrm{App}})\in(0,\infty)^{3}\times[1,\infty)^{2}
with
\beta\gamma(\kappa-1)<d\kappa
,
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1/2)
,
\tau\in[\epsilon_{0},1-\epsilon_{0}]
,
\upsilon\leq(4d^{1/2})^{-d}
and
\zeta>0
. There exist
C_{0}\geq 1
,
c_{0},c_{1}>0
, depending only on
d
,
\beta
,
\gamma
,
\kappa
,
C_{\mathrm{App}}
and
\epsilon_{0}
, such that for any
n\geq C_{0}\lambda^{d/\beta}\log(1+\zeta)
there exists a family of
L\geq\bigl\{c_{0}\bigl({n}/{\{\lambda^{d/\beta}\log(1+\zeta)}\}\bigr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}\bigr\}\vee 4
distributions

\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})

with regression functions
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{L}
and common marginal distribution
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, such that

(a)

\chi^{2}\bigl(P_{\ell}^{\otimes n},P_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\otimes n}\bigr)\leq\zeta
for all
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
;

(b)

if
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell^{\prime}})\bigr)
for some
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle M_{\tau}\bigl(P_{\ell},\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(A)\geq c_{1}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}.

(S34)

We first define some quantities for our construction. Let
\rho:={\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}
,

\displaystyle\xi_{n,\zeta,\lambda}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\epsilon_{0}\cdot(c^{\flat}_{\beta,d})^{d/\beta}}{2^{5+4d}d^{d/2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)}{n},\ \ \theta:=\xi_{n,\zeta,\lambda}^{\kappa\beta/\rho},\ \ r:=C_{\mathrm{App}}^{1/d}\cdot({8d^{1/2}})^{-1}\cdot{\theta^{\frac{\gamma(\kappa-1)}{d\kappa}}},

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle:=\big\lfloor(8d^{1/2})^{-1}\theta^{-\gamma/\kappa}\bigl\{(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr\}\big\rfloor,\ \ w:=(4d^{1/2})^{d}\cdot L\cdot\theta^{{\gamma}/{\kappa}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ s:=\Bigl(\frac{\theta}{c^{\flat}_{\beta,d}\lambda}\Bigr)^{1/\beta}.

Finally, let
C_{0}:=C_{0}^{0}\cdot(C_{0}^{1}\vee C_{0}^{2}\vee C_{0}^{3}\vee C_{0}^{4}\vee C_{0}^{5})
, where

\displaystyle C_{0}^{0}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\epsilon_{0}\cdot(c^{\flat}_{\beta,d})^{d/\beta}}{2^{5+4d}\cdot d^{d/2}},\ \ C_{0}^{1}:=\biggl(\frac{16d^{1/2}}{(c^{\flat}_{\beta,d})^{1/\beta}\cdot C_{\mathrm{App}}^{1/d}}\biggr)^{\frac{d\rho}{d\kappa-\beta\gamma(\kappa-1)}},\ \ C_{0}^{2}:=\frac{1}{(c^{\flat}_{\beta,d})^{\rho/(\kappa\beta)}},

\displaystyle C_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl(\frac{2}{\epsilon_{0}}\Bigr)^{\frac{\rho}{\kappa\beta}},\ \ C_{0}^{4}:=\biggl(\frac{C_{\mathrm{App}}}{2^{2d-5}d^{(d-1)/2}}\biggr)^{\frac{\rho}{\kappa\beta\gamma}}\quad\text{and}\quad C_{0}^{5}:=(32d^{1/2})^{\frac{\rho}{\beta\gamma}}.

Observe that when
n\geq C_{0}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)
, we have
\xi_{n,\zeta,\lambda}\leq 1/(C_{0}^{1}\vee C_{0}^{2}\vee C_{0}^{3}\vee C_{0}^{4}\vee C_{0}^{5})
. Hence, the choice of
C_{0}^{1}
ensures that
s\leq r/2
, the choice of
C_{0}^{2}
guarantees that
s\leq 1
, the choice of
C_{0}^{3}
ensures that
\theta\leq\epsilon_{0}/2
, and
C_{0}^{4}
and
C_{0}^{5}
are chosen to guarantee that

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=\bigg\lfloor 4\cdot\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{n}{C_{0}^{0}C_{0}^{4}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\rho}},\biggl(\frac{n}{C_{0}^{0}C_{0}^{5}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma}{\rho}}\biggr\}\bigg\rfloor

\displaystyle\geq\biggl\{c_{0}\cdot\biggl(\frac{n}{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}\biggr\}\vee 4,

where
c_{0}:=2\min\bigl\{(C_{0}^{0}C_{0}^{4})^{-\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\rho}},(C_{0}^{0}C_{0}^{5})^{-\frac{\beta\gamma}{\rho}}\bigr\}
. Note also that
w\leq\frac{1}{2}\big\{(2r)^{-d}\wedge 1\bigr\}
. We may therefore apply the construction following (
22
) to define distributions
P_{\ell}:=P^{\ell}_{L,r,w,s,\theta}
for
\ell\in[L]
when
n\geq C_{0}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)
. We write
\mu=\mu_{L,r,w}
and
\eta_{\ell}=\eta_{L,r,w,s,\theta}^{\ell}
in this construction.

Our choice of
s
ensures that
\theta=c^{\flat}_{\beta,d}\cdot\lambda\cdot s^{\beta}
, so we may apply Lemma
S28
to deduce that
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
for all
\ell\in[L]
. Moreover, our choice of
w
and
r
guarantee that
(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\min\big\{(w/\{(4d^{1/2})^{d}\cdot L\})^{\kappa},\theta^{\gamma}\big\}
, so we may apply Lemma
S30
to conclude that
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})\cap\mathcal{P}^{+}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
for all
\ell\in[L]
. Next, by Lemma
S31
, for each
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
,

\chi^{2}\big(P_{\ell},P_{\ell^{\prime}}\big)\leq\frac{2^{5+2d}\cdot w\cdot\theta^{2}\cdot s^{d}}{\epsilon_{0}\cdot L}=\frac{\log(1+\zeta)}{n},

by our choice of
w
,
\theta
and
s
, so

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(P_{\ell}^{\otimes n},P_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\{1+\chi^{2}\big(P_{\ell},P_{\ell^{\prime}}\big)\bigr\}^{n}-1\leq\biggl(1+\frac{\log(1+\zeta)}{n}\biggr)^{n}-1\leq\zeta.

This proves
(a)
. To prove
(b)
, we take
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
and
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell^{\prime}})\bigr)
. By Lemma
S30
, we have
M_{\tau}(P_{\ell},\mathcal{A})=\mu\big(K^{0}_{r}(\ell)\big)=(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}
. On the other hand, if
\mu(A)>0
, then since
\mathrm{supp}(\mu)=\bigcup_{(\ell^{\prime\prime},j)\in[L]\times\{0,1\}}K^{j}_{r}(\ell^{\prime\prime})
and
\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime\prime}\in[L]}K^{1}_{r}(\ell^{\prime\prime})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell^{\prime\prime}})
, we must have
A\cap K^{0}_{r}(\tilde{\ell})\neq\emptyset
for some
\tilde{\ell}\in[L]
. Since at least one of
\tilde{\ell}\neq\ell
or
\tilde{\ell}\neq\ell^{\prime}
must hold, it follows from Lemma
S30
that
\mu(A)\leq(w/L)\cdot(2r)^{d}/2
. Hence

M_{\tau}\bigl(P_{\ell},\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(A)\geq\frac{w}{L}\cdot 2^{d-1}\cdot r^{d}=\frac{C_{\mathrm{App}}}{2}\cdot\theta^{\gamma}=\frac{C_{\mathrm{App}}}{2}\biggl(\frac{C_{0}^{0}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log(1+\zeta)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}},

so (
S34
) holds with
c_{1}:=\frac{C_{\mathrm{App}}}{2}\cdot(C_{0}^{0})^{\frac{\beta\kappa\gamma}{\rho}}
.
∎

Part (i): We initially assume that
n\geq C_{0}\lambda^{d/\beta}\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)
. By Proposition
S32
, with
\zeta=1/(4\alpha)-1>0
, there exists a pair of distributions
P_{1}
,
P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
with common marginal distribution
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and corresponding regression functions
\eta_{1}
,
\eta_{2}
such that
\chi^{2}\bigl(P_{2}^{\otimes n},P_{1}^{\otimes n}\bigr)+1\leq 1/(4\alpha)
and if
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{1})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{2})\bigr)
, then

\displaystyle M_{\tau}\bigl(P_{2},\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(A)\geq c_{1}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}.

(S35)

We now define a test
\varphi:(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}\rightarrow\{1,2\}
by

\displaystyle\varphi(D):=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }\hat{A}(D)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{1})\\
2&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Since
\hat{A}
controls the Type I error at level
\alpha
over
\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
, we have

\displaystyle P_{1}^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}:\varphi(D)=2\}\bigr)=\mathbb{P}_{P_{1}}\bigl(\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{1}})\bigr)\leq\alpha.

Hence, by an immediate consequence of
Brown and Low 1996
, which we restate as Lemma
S41
for convenience, with
\epsilon=\sqrt{\alpha}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P_{2}}\bigl(\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{1}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=P_{2}^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}:\varphi(D)=1\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\Bigl\{1-\epsilon\sqrt{\chi^{2}\bigl(P_{2}^{\otimes n},P_{1}^{\otimes n}\bigr)+1}\Bigr\}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

Thus, by (
S35
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{2}}

\displaystyle\bigl[\bigl\{M_{\tau}\bigl(P,\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(\hat{A})\bigr\}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{2})\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{P_{2}}\bigl(\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{1}})\bigr\}\cap\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{2}})\bigr\}\bigr)\cdot c_{1}\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}

\displaystyle\geq\bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{P_{2}}\bigl(\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{1}})\bigr)-\mathbb{P}_{P_{2}}\bigl(\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\nsubseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}({\eta_{2}})\bigr)\bigr\}\cdot c_{1}\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}

\displaystyle\geq\biggl(\frac{1}{4}-\alpha\biggr)\cdot c_{1}\biggl(\frac{\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}}\geq\frac{c_{1}}{8}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)}{n}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}},

(S36)

as required. Moreover, if
1\leq n<C_{0}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)
, then by (
S6
) with
n\!=\!\big\lceil C_{0}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log\bigl(1/(4\alpha)\bigr)\big\rceil
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{P_{2}}\bigl[\bigl\{M_{\tau}\bigl(P,\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(\hat{A})\bigr\}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{2})\}}\bigr]\geq\frac{c_{1}}{8}\cdot C_{0}^{-\frac{\beta\gamma\kappa}{\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}},

again. This completes the proof of Part
(i)
.

Part (ii): Fix
\rho:={\kappa(2\beta+d)+\beta\gamma}
and let
C_{0}\geq 1
and
c_{0},c_{1}>0
be as in Proposition
S32
. Let
C_{1}\equiv C_{1}(d,\beta,\gamma,\kappa,C_{\mathrm{App}},\epsilon_{0})\geq e^{\frac{2\rho}{\beta\gamma}}-1
be large enough that for all
n/\lambda^{d/\beta}\geq C_{1}
, we have both
n/\lambda^{d/\beta}\geq C_{0}\log\bigl(1+(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1\}}{2\rho}}\bigr)
and

\displaystyle c_{0}\epsilon_{0}^{2}\biggl(\frac{(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})}{\log\bigl(1+(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}\bigr)}\biggr)^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{\rho}}\geq 2^{5}(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}.

Next, for
n\geq C_{1}
, we apply Proposition
S32
with
\zeta=(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}
to obtain a family of
L\geq c_{0}\bigl\{{(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})}/{\log\bigl(1+(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}\bigr)}\bigr\}^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{\rho}}\geq 2^{5}(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}/\epsilon_{0}^{2}
distributions
\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{L}\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})\cap\mathcal{P}^{+}_{\mathrm{App}}(\mathcal{A},\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
with common marginal distribution
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and corresponding regression functions
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{L}
, such that

(a)

\chi^{2}\bigl(P_{\ell}^{\otimes n},P_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\otimes n}\bigr)\leq(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}\leq({\epsilon_{0}}/4)^{2}\cdot(L-1)
for all
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
;

(b)

if
A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell^{\prime}})\bigr)
for some
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[L]
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle M_{\tau}\bigl(P_{\ell},\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(A)\geq c_{1}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\log\{1+(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}\}}{n}\biggr)^{\beta\gamma\kappa/\rho}.

(S37)

Now define a test function
\varphi:(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}\rightarrow[L]
by

\displaystyle\varphi(D):=\begin{cases}\min\{\ell\in[L]:\hat{A}(D)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell})\bigr\}&\text{ if }\hat{A}(D)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell})\text{ for some }\ell\in[L]\\
L&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

By Lemma
S42
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in[L]}P_{\ell}^{\otimes n}\bigl(\bigl\{D\in

\displaystyle(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}:\varphi(D)\neq\ell\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{L-1}\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}P_{\ell}^{\otimes n}\bigl(\bigl\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}:\varphi(D)\neq\ell\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{L-1}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{L-1}\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\chi^{2}(P_{\ell}^{\otimes n},P_{1}^{\otimes n})\cdot\frac{1}{L-1}\biggl(1-\frac{1}{L-1}\biggr)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{4}\Bigr)^{2}-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{4}\geq 1-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}.

(S38)

Now choose
\ell_{0}\in[L]
with
P_{\ell_{0}}^{\otimes n}\bigl(\bigl\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[0,1])^{n}:\varphi(D)\neq\ell_{0}\bigr\}\bigr)\geq 1-\epsilon_{0}/2
, and observe that if
\varphi(D)\neq\ell_{0}
and
\hat{A}(D)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{0}})
then we must also have
\hat{A}(D)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{1}})
for some
\ell_{1}\in[\ell_{0}-1]
. It follows from this and (
S6
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P_{\ell_{0}}}\biggl(\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{0}})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\cap\bigcup_{\ell_{1}=1}^{\ell_{0}-1}\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{1}})\bigr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{P_{\ell_{0}}}\bigl(\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{0}})\bigr\}\cap\bigl\{\varphi(\mathcal{D})\neq\ell_{0}\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq P_{\ell_{0}}^{\otimes n}\bigl(\bigl\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\!\times\![0,1])^{n}:\varphi(D)\neq\ell_{0}\bigr\}\bigr)-\alpha\geq\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}.

Thus, by (
S37
), we have for all
n\geq C_{1}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\geq e^{\frac{2\rho}{\beta\gamma}}-1
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{\ell_{0}}}\bigl[\bigl\{M_{\tau}\bigl(P_{\ell_{0}},\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu(\hat{A})\bigr\}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{0}})\}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\mathbb{P}_{P_{\ell_{0}}}\biggl(\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{0}})\bigr\}\cap\bigcup_{\ell_{1}=1}^{\ell_{0}-1}\bigl\{\hat{A}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta_{\ell_{1}})\bigr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot c_{1}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{d/\beta}\log\{1+(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})^{\frac{\beta\gamma(\kappa\wedge 1)}{2\rho}}\}}{n}\biggr)^{\beta\gamma\kappa/\rho}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\frac{c_{1}\epsilon_{0}}{2}\cdot\biggl(\frac{{\beta\gamma}(\kappa\wedge 1)\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}\cdot\log_{+}(n/\lambda^{d/\beta})}{{2\rho}n}\biggr)^{\beta\gamma\kappa/\rho}.

We extend the bound to
n<C_{1}\cdot\lambda^{d/\beta}
by monotonicity as at the end of the proof of Proposition
14
(i)
, with

c_{2}:=\frac{c_{1}\epsilon_{0}}{2}\cdot\biggl(\frac{{\beta\gamma}(\kappa\wedge 1)\log_{+}\lceil C_{1}\rceil}{2\rho\lceil C_{1}\rceil}\biggr)^{\beta\gamma\kappa/\rho},

which completes the proof.
∎Finally, we prove the parametric lower bounds in Theorems
2
and
11
. Some care is required here to show that our constructed distributions belong to the relevant distributional classes.

Observe that there exists
c_{\mathrm{H}}\equiv c_{\mathrm{H}}(\beta)\in(0,1]
such that when
\theta\leq c_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\lambda\cdot s^{\beta}
, we have that
\eta
is
(\beta,\lambda)
-Hölder, so
\{P_{\zeta}^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\{-1,1\}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H\ddot{o}l}}(\beta,\lambda)
. In addition,
\mathrm{supp}(\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)=A_{-1}\cup A_{1}
for
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
. Since
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{conv}}
and
A_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
, it follows that for
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
,

M_{\tau}(P_{\zeta}^{\ell},\mathcal{A})=\max_{j\in\{-1,1\}}\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A_{j})=\frac{s^{d}}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}+\zeta.

Observe also that for any
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
,
x\in A_{\ell}
and
r\in(0,4s]
, we have

\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r}(x)\bigr)\geq\biggl(\frac{1}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}+\frac{\zeta}{s^{d}}\biggr)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{d}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r/4}(x)\cap A_{\ell}\bigr)\geq\biggl(\frac{1}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}+\frac{\zeta}{s^{d}}\biggr)\cdot\biggl(\frac{r}{4}\biggr)^{d}.

Moreover, for any
\ell\in\{-1,1\}
,
x\in A_{\ell}
and
r\in(4s,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r}(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathcal{L}_{d}\bigl(\bar{B}_{r}(x)\cap A_{0}\bigr)}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}\geq\frac{r^{d-1}\cdot(r-2s)}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{r^{d}}{2\{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}\}}\geq\biggl(\frac{1}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}+\frac{\zeta}{s^{d}}\biggr)\cdot\frac{r^{d}}{3}.

Hence,
\omega_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell},d}(x)\geq\{(8t)^{d}+2(4s)^{d}\}^{-1}
for all
x\in A_{\ell}
, and moreover
A_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell})
. Thus, for any
(\xi,\Delta)\in\bigl(0,\{(8t)^{d}+2(4s)^{d}\}^{-1}\bigr]\times(0,\theta]
, we have

\sup\bigl\{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell},d})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}=\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A_{\ell})=M_{\tau}(P_{\zeta}^{\ell},\mathcal{A}),

and similarly,

\sup\bigl\{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu^{\ell}_{\zeta})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}=\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A_{\ell})=M_{\tau}(P_{\zeta}^{\ell},\mathcal{A}).

On the other hand, if either
\xi>\{(8t)^{d}+2(4s)^{d}\}^{-1}
or
\Delta>\theta
, then provided that
\frac{3s^{d}}{2\{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}\}}\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\bigl[\{(8t)^{d}+2(4s)^{d}\}^{-\kappa}\wedge\theta^{\gamma}\bigr]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup\bigl\{

\displaystyle\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{R}_{\upsilon}(\mu^{\ell}_{\zeta})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\wedge\sup\bigl\{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(A):A\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathrm{Pow}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{\xi}(\omega_{\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell},d})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\tau+\Delta}(\eta)\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq 0\geq M_{\tau}(P_{\zeta}^{\ell},\mathcal{A})-C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot(\xi^{\kappa}+\Delta^{\gamma}).

It follows that
\{P_{\zeta}^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\{-1,1\}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A},\beta,\kappa,\gamma,\upsilon,\lambda,\tau,C_{\mathrm{App}})
whenever
\frac{3s^{d}}{2\{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}\}}\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\bigl[\{(8t)^{d}+2(4s)^{d}\}^{-\kappa}\wedge\theta^{\gamma}\bigr]
.

In addition, recalling that
m_{\mathrm{ct}}
denotes the counting measure, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\bigl(P_{\zeta}^{-1},P_{\zeta}^{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\chi^{2}\bigl(P_{\zeta}^{-1},P_{\zeta}^{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}}\frac{\bigl\{f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}}(x)-f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{1}}(x)\bigr\}^{2}\eta(x)^{y}\{1-\eta(x)\}^{1-y}}{f_{\mu_{\zeta}^{1}}(x)}\,d(\mathcal{L}_{d}\times m_{\mathrm{ct}})(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\zeta/s^{d})^{2}}{\frac{1}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}-\frac{\zeta}{s^{d}}}+\frac{(2\zeta/s^{d})^{2}}{\frac{1}{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}+\frac{\zeta}{s^{d}}}\leq\frac{32}{3}\cdot\frac{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}{s^{2d}}\cdot\zeta^{2}.

We deduce by Pinsker’s inequality that

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl((P_{\zeta}^{-1})^{\otimes n},(P_{\zeta}^{1})^{\otimes n}\bigr)\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{KL}\bigl((P_{\zeta}^{-1})^{\otimes n},(P_{\zeta}^{+1})^{\otimes n}\bigr)/2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\zeta\sqrt{n}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}{3s^{2d}}}

\displaystyle=2a\cdot\zeta\sqrt{n},

where
a\equiv a_{d,s,t}:=2\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}}{3s^{2d}}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=(P_{\zeta}^{1})^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\})^{n}:A_{1}\cap\hat{A}(D)=\emptyset\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+(P_{\zeta}^{1})^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\})^{n}:A_{1}\cap\hat{A}(D)\neq\emptyset\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq(P_{\zeta}^{1})^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D:A_{1}\cap\hat{A}(D)=\emptyset\}\bigr)+(P_{\zeta}^{-1})^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D:A_{1}\cap\hat{A}(D)\neq\emptyset\}\bigr)+2a\cdot\zeta\sqrt{n}.

Hence, since at most one of
A_{-1}
and
A_{1}
can have non-empty intersection with
\hat{A}
whenever
\hat{A}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in\{-1,1\}}(P_{\zeta}^{\ell})^{\otimes n}\bigl(\{D:A_{\ell}\cap\hat{A}(D)=\emptyset\}\cap\bigl\{D:\hat{A}(D)\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-a\cdot\zeta\sqrt{n}-\alpha

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{0}-a\cdot\zeta\sqrt{n}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in\{-1,1\}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{\zeta}^{\ell}}\bigl[\bigl\{M_{\tau}\bigl(P_{\zeta}^{\ell},\mathcal{A}\bigr)-\mu_{\zeta}^{\ell}(\hat{A})\bigr\}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{A}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(\eta)\}}\bigr]\geq 2\zeta\bigl(\epsilon_{0}-a\cdot\zeta\sqrt{n}\bigr).

Taking
t:=\bigl\{\bigl(2^{\kappa(3d+1)}\vee(c_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\lambda)^{\gamma}\bigr)/C_{\mathrm{App}}\bigr\}^{\frac{\beta\gamma}{d(d\kappa-\beta\gamma(\kappa-1))}}\vee 1
,
s:=t^{-\frac{d\kappa}{\beta\gamma}}
and

\theta:=c_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\lambda\cdot s^{\beta}
ensures that the conditions
\frac{3s^{d}}{2\{(2t)^{d}+2s^{d}\}}\leq C_{\mathrm{App}}\cdot\bigl[\{(8t)^{d}+2(4s)^{d}\}^{-\kappa}\wedge\bigl(c_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\lambda\cdot s^{\beta}\bigr)^{\gamma}\bigr]
and
\theta\leq c_{\mathrm{H}}\cdot\lambda\cdot s^{\beta}
hold. Hence, taking
\zeta:=\epsilon_{0}/(2a\sqrt{n})
yields the required lower bound.
∎

In light of the remarks following Proposition
14
, these results follow from Propositions
14
and
15
.
∎

S7
Parameter constraints

The following lemma reveals natural constraints satisfied by the parameters
\beta
,
\kappa
and
\gamma
.

Lemma S33
.

Take
